| Command | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| [`c1i access-profiles list`](#access-profiles-list) | List access profiles. |
| [`c1i access-profiles get`](#access-profiles-get) | Get a single access profile by ID. |
| [`c1i access-profiles create`](#access-profiles-create) | Create an access profile. |
| [`c1i access-profiles update`](#access-profiles-update) | Update an access profile. |
| [`c1i access-profiles delete`](#access-profiles-delete) | Soft-delete an access profile by ID. |
| `c1i access-profiles requestable-entitlements list <access-profile-id>` | List entitlements users can request from an access profile. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| `c1i access-profiles requestable-entitlements list-ids <access-profile-id>` | List requestable entitlement references (app ID + entitlement ID pairs) for an access profile. |
| [`c1i access-profiles requestable-entitlements add`](#access-profiles-requestable-entitlements-add) | Add requestable entitlements to an access profile. |
| [`c1i access-profiles requestable-entitlements remove`](#access-profiles-requestable-entitlements-remove) | Remove requestable entitlements from an access profile. |
| [`c1i access-profiles requestable-entitlements set`](#access-profiles-requestable-entitlements-set) | Replace the full requestable-entitlement set in an access profile. |
| `c1i access-profiles visibility-entitlements list <access-profile-id>` | List entitlements required to see an access profile. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| [`c1i access-profiles visibility-entitlements add`](#access-profiles-visibility-entitlements-add) | Add visibility entitlements to an access profile. |
| [`c1i access-profiles visibility-entitlements remove`](#access-profiles-visibility-entitlements-remove) | Remove visibility entitlements from an access profile. |
| `c1i access-profiles bundle-automation get <access-profile-id>` | Get an access profile's bundle automation. |
| [`c1i access-profiles bundle-automation create`](#access-profiles-bundle-automation-create) | Create an access profile's bundle automation. |
| [`c1i access-profiles bundle-automation set`](#access-profiles-bundle-automation-set) | Replace an access profile's bundle automation. |
| `c1i access-profiles bundle-automation delete <access-profile-id>` | Delete an access profile's bundle automation. |
| `c1i access-profiles bundle-automation resume <access-profile-id>` | Resume a paused access profile bundle automation. |
| [`c1i access-profiles bundle-automation run`](#access-profiles-bundle-automation-run) | Run an access profile's bundle automation. |

<Note>
A profile's visibility bindings are meant to be added after it's published: adding an access entitlement to an unpublished profile can be refused with a 400 (`catalog must be published to add an access entitlement`), and adding one to a profile that's `--visible-to-everyone` is refused with a 400 (`catalog is visible to everyone, cannot add access entitlements`). Publish first if you plan to restrict visibility by entitlement. The unpublished-profile refusal is not an enforced guard: in one measured run both a requestable and a visibility add on an unpublished profile returned `200 {}` and the binding was stored, so treat publishing first as required for the binding to take effect, and check the result with `access-profiles get`.
</Note>

`requestable-entitlements add`/`remove` and `visibility-entitlements add`/`remove` take a shared `--app-id` plus one or more repeatable `--entitlement-id` flags, since an entitlement reference pairs an entitlement ID with the application that owns it. `requestable-entitlements set` instead takes a `--refs-file` JSON array of `{"appId":"...","id":"..."}` objects (file, or `-` for stdin), since it replaces the entire set rather than adding to it.

### `access-profiles list`

[Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

Rows don't carry a member count — the list endpoint reports it as `0` for every profile and takes no parameter that would populate it. Use `get` for a real count.

### `access-profiles get`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-profiles get <access-profile-id>
```

Unwraps the API's `requestCatalogView.requestCatalog` envelope, so the profile's own keys (`id`, `displayName`, `published`, …) sit at the top level beside the view's own siblings (`memberCount`, `accessEntitlementsPath`, `createdByUserPath`). A `get` also returns the profile's `accessEntitlements` (its visibility bindings), which `list` rows leave out.

### `access-profiles create`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-profiles create --display-name <name> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--display-name string` | Display name for the new access profile. (Required.) |
| `--description string` | Description for the new access profile. |
| `--published` | Create the access profile already published. |
| `--visible-to-everyone` | Let every user see the access profile regardless of its access entitlements; while set, the API refuses to add new ones (`catalog is visible to everyone, cannot add access entitlements`). |
| `--request-bundle` | Allow requesting every entitlement in the profile at once. Requires the bundles feature on the tenant. |
| `--enrollment-behavior string` | Enrollment request-policy behavior: `bypass`, `enforce`, `unspecified`, or a full `REQUEST_CATALOG_ENROLLMENT_BEHAVIOR_*` enum value. |
| `--unenrollment-behavior string` | Unenrollment behavior: `leave-access-as-is`, `revoke-all`, `revoke-unjustified`, `unspecified`, or a full `REQUEST_CATALOG_UNENROLLMENT_BEHAVIOR_*` enum value. |
| `--unenrollment-entitlement-behavior string` | Unenrollment entitlement revoke-policy behavior: `bypass`, `enforce`, `unspecified`, or a full `REQUEST_CATALOG_UNENROLLMENT_ENTITLEMENT_BEHAVIOR_*` enum value. |

Only `--display-name` is required; every other field is omitted from the request unless you pass it, so the server's own defaults apply. c1i prints the created profile unwrapped like `get`, so read the ID from `.id` and the member count from `.memberCount`.

**Example:**

```bash
CAT_ID=$(c1i access-profiles create --display-name "Engineering" --published | jq -er .id)
```

### `access-profiles update`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-profiles update <access-profile-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--display-name string` | New display name. |
| `--description string` | New description; pass an empty value to clear it. |
| `--published` | Publish or unpublish the access profile. |
| `--visible-to-everyone` | Set whether every user can see the access profile. |
| `--request-bundle` | Set whether all profile entitlements can be requested together. |
| `--enrollment-behavior string` | Enrollment request-policy behavior (same values as `create`). |
| `--unenrollment-behavior string` | Unenrollment behavior (same values as `create`). |
| `--unenrollment-entitlement-behavior string` | Unenrollment entitlement revoke-policy behavior (same values as `create`). |

This is a partial update: only the fields you pass are changed, and the update mask is derived automatically from which flags you supplied — there's no separate `--update-mask` flag here. An explicit `--description ""` is itself a change (it clears the description); an omitted flag is left alone. The updated profile is printed unwrapped, like `create`.

### `access-profiles delete`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-profiles delete <access-profile-id>
```

The profile is retained for audit: it drops out of `list`, but `get` still returns it with `deletedAt` populated.

### `access-profiles requestable-entitlements add`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-profiles requestable-entitlements add <access-profile-id> \
  --app-id <app-id> --entitlement-id <entitlement-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID that owns the entitlements. (Required.) |
| `--entitlement-id stringArray` | Entitlement ID to add (repeatable; at least one required). |
| `--create-requests` | Also create requests for users already in the access profile for these entitlements. |

### `access-profiles requestable-entitlements remove`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-profiles requestable-entitlements remove <access-profile-id> \
  --app-id <app-id> --entitlement-id <entitlement-id>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID that owns the entitlements. (Required.) |
| `--entitlement-id stringArray` | Entitlement ID to remove (repeatable; at least one required). |

### `access-profiles requestable-entitlements set`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-profiles requestable-entitlements set <access-profile-id> \
  --refs-file <file>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--refs-file string` | JSON array of `{"appId":"...","id":"..."}` objects (file, or `-` for stdin). (Required.) |

Every existing requestable entitlement not in the array is removed; pass `[]` to clear the entire set.

### `access-profiles visibility-entitlements add`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-profiles visibility-entitlements add <access-profile-id> \
  --app-id <app-id> --entitlement-id <entitlement-id>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID that owns the entitlements. (Required.) |
| `--entitlement-id stringArray` | Entitlement ID to add (repeatable; at least one required). |

### `access-profiles visibility-entitlements remove`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-profiles visibility-entitlements remove <access-profile-id> \
  --app-id <app-id> --entitlement-id <entitlement-id>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID that owns the entitlements. (Required.) |
| `--entitlement-id stringArray` | Entitlement ID to remove (repeatable; at least one required). |

### `access-profiles bundle-automation create`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-profiles bundle-automation create <access-profile-id> \
  --body-file <file>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--body-file string` | Bundle automation JSON object (file, or `-` for stdin). (Required.) |

The supported fields are `createTasks`, `disableCircuitBreaker`, and `enabled` (all booleans), and `entitlements` (an object with `entitlementRefs`, an array of `{"appId":"...","id":"..."}` objects).

### `access-profiles bundle-automation set`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-profiles bundle-automation set <access-profile-id> --body-file <file>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--body-file string` | Bundle automation JSON object (file, or `-` for stdin). (Required.) |

Takes the same fields as `bundle-automation create`.

### `access-profiles bundle-automation run`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-profiles bundle-automation run <access-profile-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--refs-file string` | JSON array of `{"appId":"...","id":"..."}` objects to run (file, or `-` for stdin). Omit to run against the automation's configured entitlements. |

## Access reviews

Manage access-review campaigns — the governance campaigns the API calls access reviews — and the templates they are built from. A campaign defines a scope of users and entitlements to certify, who reviews them, and reports export the campaign's results.

`create` takes the complete request body as JSON, since campaign scope is a large nested oneof shape; `update` takes a partial access-review object plus an explicit `--update-mask`.

| Command | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `c1i access-reviews list` | List access-review campaigns. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| `c1i access-reviews get <campaign-id>` | Get an access-review campaign by ID. |
| [`c1i access-reviews create`](#access-reviews-create) | Create an access-review campaign from a complete JSON request object. |
| [`c1i access-reviews update`](#access-reviews-update) | Update an access-review campaign from a partial JSON object. |
| [`c1i access-reviews delete`](#access-reviews-delete) | Soft-delete an access-review campaign. |
| [`c1i access-reviews scope get`](#access-reviews-scope-get) | Read a campaign's scope, selected entitlements, and exclusions. |
| [`c1i access-reviews scope set`](#access-reviews-scope-set) | Replace a campaign's scope and its whole entitlement selection. |
| [`c1i access-reviews scope set-excluded`](#access-reviews-scope-set-excluded) | Replace a campaign's scope and its whole excluded-entitlement set. |
| [`c1i access-reviews scope set-by-resource-type`](#access-reviews-scope-set-by-resource-type) | Replace a campaign's scope and its resource-type selection. |
| [`c1i access-reviews entitlements add`](#access-reviews-entitlements-add) | Add entitlements to a campaign's selection. |
| [`c1i access-reviews entitlements remove`](#access-reviews-entitlements-remove) | Remove entitlements from a campaign's selection. |
| [`c1i access-reviews excluded-entitlements add`](#access-reviews-excluded-entitlements-add) | Add entitlements to a campaign's excluded set. |
| [`c1i access-reviews excluded-entitlements remove`](#access-reviews-excluded-entitlements-remove) | Remove entitlements from a campaign's excluded set. |
| `c1i access-reviews reports list <campaign-id>` | List an access-review campaign's generated reports. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| [`c1i access-reviews reports generate`](#access-reviews-reports-generate) | Request asynchronous generation of an access-review report. |
| [`c1i access-reviews templates`](#access-reviews-templates) | Manage reusable campaign configurations. |

Most of the scope routes are marked draft in the API spec, so treat their shapes as provisional.

### `access-reviews create`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-reviews create --body-file <file>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--body-file string` | Full create-access-review request JSON object (file, or `-` for stdin). (Required.) |

The request must contain at least one owner ID in `ownerIds` and a `policyId`; the server also requires a 27-character policy ID. The created campaign is printed unwrapped like `get`, so its ID is `.id`.

### `access-reviews update`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-reviews update <campaign-id> --body-file <file> \
  --update-mask <fields>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--body-file string` | Partial access-review JSON object (file, or `-` for stdin). Must not contain `id` (or must equal `<campaign-id>`). (Required.) |
| `--update-mask string` | Comma-separated campaign fields to update. (Required.) |

The command inserts the campaign ID into the body and wraps the request; both flags are required. It prints the campaign unwrapped like `get`.

### `access-reviews delete`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-reviews delete <campaign-id>
```

The API documents that this transitions the campaign, along with its dependent objects, to the deleted state. The campaign leaves `access-reviews list`, but `access-reviews get` still returns it with state `ACCESS_REVIEW_STATE_DELETED` — the model has no `deletedAt`, so check `state`. Deleting it again exits `0` again. Prints `{}`; exit `0` is the success signal.

### `access-reviews scope get`

Read a campaign's scope and entitlement setup as one JSON object: the selected entitlements (`list`), the scope, `excludedEntitlements`, and `excludedResourceTypes`. The response is not paginated.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-reviews scope get <campaign-id>
```

### `access-reviews scope set`

Replace a campaign's `scopeV2` and its whole entitlement selection from a JSON request object:

```json
{"scopeV2": {}, "entitlements": [{"appId": "…", "appEntitlementId": "…"}]}
```

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-reviews scope set <campaign-id> --body-file <file>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--body-file string` | Full request JSON object (file, or `-` for stdin). Must not contain `accessReviewId` or `access_review_id` (or must equal `<campaign-id>`). (Required.) |

Each call replaces the whole selection, so to change only some entitlements use `entitlements add`/`remove`. The response's `list` holds only the entitlements this call added, because the server returns the delta — follow with `scope get` to read the resulting selection.

Each referenced entitlement is looked up before sending, `--dry-run` included: an unknown one exits `4` and a soft-deleted one exits `2`, with nothing sent.

### `access-reviews scope set-excluded`

Replace a campaign's `scopeV2` and its whole excluded-entitlement set:

```json
{"scopeV2": {"excludedSpecificResources": {}},
 "excludedEntitlements": [{"appId": "…", "appEntitlementId": "…"}]}
```

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-reviews scope set-excluded <campaign-id> --body-file <file>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--body-file string` | Full request JSON object (file, or `-` for stdin). Must not contain `accessReviewId` or `access_review_id` (or must equal `<campaign-id>`). (Required.) |

Exclusions apply only while `scopeV2` sets `excludedSpecificResources`. The API documents support for `BY_USERS`, selector/resource-type `BY_ENTITLEMENTS`, `BY_INHERITANCE`, and `BY_ACCESS_CONFLICTS` campaigns; another scope is refused with `exclusions are not supported for this access review scope` (exit `2`). To exclude by resource type, set `scopeV2.excludedAppSelectionCriteria` here: the API's separate scope-by-excluded-resource-type route is superseded and scheduled for removal, so c1i has no command for it. Prints `{"excludedEntitlements": [...], "scopeV2": {...}}`.

### `access-reviews scope set-by-resource-type`

Replace a campaign's `scopeV2` and its resource-type selection:

```json
{"scopeV2": {"resourceTypeSelections": {}},
 "resourceTypeSelections": [{"appId": "…", "resourceTypeId": "…"}]}
```

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-reviews scope set-by-resource-type <campaign-id> --body-file <file>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--body-file string` | Full request JSON object (file, or `-` for stdin). Must not contain `accessReviewId` or `access_review_id` (or must equal `<campaign-id>`). (Required.) |

The list replaces all previously selected resource types. Prints `{}`. In the run checked, `scope get` did not list the selected resource types back.

### `access-reviews entitlements add`

Add one application's entitlements to a campaign's selection without replacing the rest. An entitlement already selected is skipped, and the server takes 1 to 200 per call.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-reviews entitlements add <campaign-id> \
  --app-id <app-id> --entitlement-id <entitlement-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID that owns the entitlements. (Required.) |
| `--entitlement-id stringArray` | Application entitlement ID (repeatable). (Required.) |

A campaign with no scope yet gets `scopeV2.specificResources`; one with another scope is refused (`campaign entitlements can only be added or removed when the campaign's apps and resources scope is set to specific entitlements`, exit `2`), so change it with `scope set` first. Prints `{"entitlementCount": N}`, the selection's size after the call.

### `access-reviews entitlements remove`

Remove one application's entitlements from a campaign's selection. An entitlement that is not selected is ignored. Like `add`, it needs a campaign whose `scopeV2` sets `specificResources`.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-reviews entitlements remove <campaign-id> \
  --app-id <app-id> --entitlement-id <entitlement-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID that owns the entitlements. (Required.) |
| `--entitlement-id stringArray` | Application entitlement ID (repeatable). (Required.) |

Prints `{"entitlementCount": N, "removedCount": M}`: the selection's size after the call and how many of the given entitlements were removed.

### `access-reviews excluded-entitlements add`

Add one application's entitlements to a campaign's excluded set. An entitlement already excluded is skipped. The campaign's `scopeV2` must set `excludedSpecificResources` first, or the server answers `excluded_specific_resources must be active before adding excluded entitlements` (exit `2`); set it with `scope set-excluded`.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-reviews excluded-entitlements add <campaign-id> \
  --app-id <app-id> --entitlement-id <entitlement-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID that owns the entitlements. (Required.) |
| `--entitlement-id stringArray` | Application entitlement ID (repeatable). (Required.) |

Prints `{}`.

### `access-reviews excluded-entitlements remove`

Remove one application's entitlements from a campaign's excluded set; one that is not excluded is ignored. In the run checked, removing the last excluded entitlement also cleared `scopeV2.excludedSpecificResources`, so a later `excluded-entitlements add` needed `scope set-excluded` first.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-reviews excluded-entitlements remove <campaign-id> \
  --app-id <app-id> --entitlement-id <entitlement-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID that owns the entitlements. (Required.) |
| `--entitlement-id stringArray` | Application entitlement ID (repeatable). (Required.) |

Prints `{}`.

### `access-reviews reports generate`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-reviews reports generate <campaign-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--format string` | Report format: `json`, `csv`, `xlsx`, or the full `ACCESS_REVIEW_REPORT_FORMAT_*` name of one. Mutually exclusive with `--body-file`. |
| `--body-file string` | Full report-generation request JSON object (file, or `-` for stdin). Mutually exclusive with `--format`. |

The command prints `{}`, which confirms that generation was requested, not that the report is ready — use `reports list` to find the report's state and its time-limited `downloadUrl`.

### `access-reviews templates`

Manage access-review templates: reusable campaign configurations covering owners, review policy, scope, and schedule.

The API has no route that lists or searches templates, so an ID comes from `templates create` (its `.id`) or from a campaign made from a template — `access-reviews list` rows carry it as `accessReviewTemplateId`.

| Command | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `c1i access-reviews templates get <template-id>` | Get an access-review template by ID. |
| [`c1i access-reviews templates create`](#access-reviews-templates-create) | Create an access-review template. |
| [`c1i access-reviews templates update`](#access-reviews-templates-update) | Update an access-review template's display name, description, instructions, policy, or scope type. |
| `c1i access-reviews templates delete <template-id>` | Delete an access-review template. |
| [`c1i access-reviews templates scope get`](#access-reviews-templates-scope-get) | Read a template's scope, selected entitlements, and exclusions. |
| [`c1i access-reviews templates scope set`](#access-reviews-templates-scope-set) | Replace a template's scope and its whole entitlement selection. |
| [`c1i access-reviews templates scope set-excluded`](#access-reviews-templates-scope-set-excluded) | Replace a template's scope and its whole excluded-entitlement set. |
| [`c1i access-reviews templates scope set-by-resource-type`](#access-reviews-templates-scope-set-by-resource-type) | Replace a template's scope and its resource-type selection. |
| [`c1i access-reviews templates entitlements add`](#access-reviews-templates-entitlements-add) | Add entitlements to a template's selection; `remove` takes the same flags. |
| [`c1i access-reviews templates excluded-entitlements add`](#access-reviews-templates-excluded-entitlements-add) | Add entitlements to a template's excluded set; `remove` takes the same flags. |

### `access-reviews templates create`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-reviews templates create --display-name <name> \
  --policy-id <policy-id> --owner-id <user-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--display-name string` | Display name. Can't be blank. (Required unless `--body-file`.) |
| `--policy-id string` | Review policy ID; must exist and be a certify policy. (Required unless `--body-file`.) |
| `--owner-id stringArray` | Owner user ID (repeatable). (Required unless `--body-file`.) |
| `--description string` | Description. |
| `--review-instructions string` | Instructions shown to reviewers. |
| `--scope-type string` | Scope type: `by-entitlements`, `by-access-conflicts`, `by-resource`, `by-inheritance`, `by-users`, `by-access-profiles`, or any `ACCESS_REVIEW_SCOPE_TYPE_*` value. |
| `--body-file string` | Full create request body, verbatim (file, or `-` for stdin); mutually exclusive with every other flag. |

The server requires a display name, a review policy, and 1 to 10 owners. Omitting `--scope-type` left the server default `ACCESS_REVIEW_SCOPE_TYPE_BY_ENTITLEMENTS` in the run checked. The flags create a template with no scope, no schedule, and auto-start off; set the scope with the `scope` and `entitlements` verbs below. `--body-file` covers what the flags don't: `recurrenceRule`, `isCampaignScheduleEnabled`, `autoStartCampaign`, notification and column config, and so on; its `ownerIds` and `policyId` are looked up like the flags'.

The policy and every owner are looked up first, `--dry-run` included: an unknown ID exits `4`, and a deleted or non-certify policy exits `2`, before anything is sent. Prints the template unwrapped like `templates get`.

### `access-reviews templates update`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-reviews templates update <template-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--display-name string` | New display name. Omit to leave unchanged; can't be blank. |
| `--description string` | New description; `""` is sent as an empty value. |
| `--review-instructions string` | New reviewer instructions; `""` is sent as an empty value. |
| `--policy-id string` | New review policy ID; must exist and be a certify policy. |
| `--scope-type string` | New scope type (same values as `create`). |
| `--body-file string` | Access-review-template JSON object (file, or `-` for stdin); requires `--update-mask`; mutually exclusive with the flags above. |
| `--update-mask string` | Comma-separated field paths (required with `--body-file`; rejected without it; derived otherwise). |

The mask is derived from the flags you pass. The server validates `displayName` and `policyId` on every update whatever the mask names, so when the request leaves either unset c1i reads the template first and fills it in; the mask still decides what changes. A mask that names either one needs its value in `--body-file`, else exit `2` with nothing sent. That read makes an unknown `<template-id>` exit `4` before the update, `--dry-run` included.

For anything else — schedule, recurrence, notification config — pass a template object with `--body-file` plus an explicit `--update-mask`; the object is sent with `id` set to `<template-id>`. A `policyId` is looked up only when the mask names it.

### `access-reviews templates get` and `access-reviews templates delete`

`get` prints the template unwrapped, so its ID is `.id`. In the run checked a deleted template was still returned, with `deletedAt` null.

`delete` sends the API's documented request — "The template can no longer be used to create campaigns" — and prints `{}`. An unknown ID exits `4`; deleting again exits `0`. Exit `0` only means the API accepted the request: in the run checked the template stayed readable with `deletedAt` null.

### `access-reviews templates scope get`

Read a template's scope and entitlement setup as one JSON object: the selected entitlements (`list`), the scope, `excludedEntitlements`, and `excludedResourceTypes`. The response is not paginated.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-reviews templates scope get <template-id>
```

### `access-reviews templates scope set`

Replace a template's scope and its whole entitlement selection:

```json
{"scope": {}, "entitlements": [{"appId": "…", "appEntitlementId": "…"}]}
```

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-reviews templates scope set <template-id> --body-file <file>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--body-file string` | Full request JSON object (file, or `-` for stdin). Must not contain `accessReviewTemplateId` or `access_review_template_id` (or must equal `<template-id>`). (Required.) |

Each call replaces the whole selection. The response's `list` holds only the entitlements this call added, so follow with `scope get` to read the result.

### `access-reviews templates scope set-excluded`

Replace a template's scope and its whole excluded-entitlement set:

```json
{"scope": {"excludedSpecificResources": {}},
 "excludedEntitlements": [{"appId": "…", "appEntitlementId": "…"}]}
```

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-reviews templates scope set-excluded <template-id> --body-file <file>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--body-file string` | Full request JSON object (file, or `-` for stdin). Must not contain `accessReviewTemplateId` or `access_review_template_id` (or must equal `<template-id>`). (Required.) |

Exclusions apply only while the scope sets `excludedSpecificResources`, and the same scope types as the campaign route are supported; another scope is refused with `exclusions are not supported for this access review scope` (exit `2`). To exclude by resource type, set `scope.excludedAppSelectionCriteria` here. Prints `{"excludedEntitlements": [...], "scope": {...}}`.

### `access-reviews templates scope set-by-resource-type`

Replace a template's scope and its resource-type selection:

```json
{"scope": {"resourceTypeSelections": {}},
 "resourceTypeSelections": [{"appId": "…", "resourceTypeId": "…"}]}
```

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-reviews templates scope set-by-resource-type <template-id> --body-file <file>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--body-file string` | Full request JSON object (file, or `-` for stdin). Must not contain `accessReviewTemplateId` or `access_review_template_id` (or must equal `<template-id>`). (Required.) |

Prints `{}`. In the run checked, `templates scope get` did not list the selected resource types back.

### `access-reviews templates entitlements add`

Add one application's entitlements to a template's selection without replacing the rest. An entitlement already selected is skipped, and the server takes 1 to 200 per call.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-reviews templates entitlements add <template-id> \
  --app-id <app-id> --entitlement-id <entitlement-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID that owns the entitlements. (Required.) |
| `--entitlement-id stringArray` | Application entitlement ID (repeatable). (Required.) |

A template with no scope yet gets `scope.specificResources`; one with another scope is refused (`template entitlements can only be added or removed when the template's apps and resources scope is set to specific entitlements`, exit `2`). Prints `{"entitlementCount": N}`. `c1i access-reviews templates entitlements remove` takes the same flags and prints `{"entitlementCount": N, "removedCount": M}`.

### `access-reviews templates excluded-entitlements add`

Add one application's entitlements to a template's excluded set. The template's scope must set `excludedSpecificResources` first, or the server answers `excluded_specific_resources must be active before adding excluded entitlements` (exit `2`); set it with `templates scope set-excluded`.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i access-reviews templates excluded-entitlements add <template-id> \
  --app-id <app-id> --entitlement-id <entitlement-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID that owns the entitlements. (Required.) |
| `--entitlement-id stringArray` | Application entitlement ID (repeatable). (Required.) |

`c1i access-reviews templates excluded-entitlements remove` takes the same flags. In the run checked, removing the last excluded entitlement also cleared `scope.excludedSpecificResources`, so a later `add` needed `templates scope set-excluded` first. Both print `{}`.

<Note>
Every template and campaign scope verb looks up each referenced entitlement, resource type, policy, and user before sending, `--dry-run` included: an unknown ID exits `4`, a soft-deleted object exits `2`. `--fields` is a usage error on these mutations; extract IDs with `jq -er`, which fails on a `--dry-run` preview.
</Note>

## Findings

Manage security findings and the public API surfaces that govern them: routing rules, transformation rules, detector settings (including the Edge categories), and the audit/occurrence search surfaces behind them.

`create`, `state`, `assignee`, the bulk commands, routing/transformation-rule management, and the settings updates all take JSON files so oneof actions and nested rule documents reach the API unchanged. Pass `--body-file -` to read JSON from standard input.

| Command | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| [`c1i findings search`](#findings-search) | Search findings with an optional JSON query object. |
| `c1i findings get <finding-id>` | Get a finding by ID. |
| `c1i findings create --body-file <file>` | Create a custom finding from a JSON finding object. |
| `c1i findings state <finding-id> --body-file <file>` | Change a finding's state from a JSON state-change object. |
| `c1i findings assignee <finding-id> --body-file <file>` | Assign or clear a finding's assignee from a JSON assignee object. |
| `c1i findings create-task <finding-id> [--policy-id <policy-id>]` | Create a remediation task for a finding, optionally with a specific remediation policy. |
| `c1i findings bulk-state --body-file <file>` | Run an asynchronous bulk state-change operation from a JSON object. |
| `c1i findings bulk-create-tasks --body-file <file>` | Create remediation tasks in bulk from a JSON object. |
| `c1i findings routing-rules list [--limit <n>]` | List finding routing rules. Returns a single, fixed-size page; there's no `--page-size` or `--page-token`. |
| `c1i findings routing-rules get <rule-id>` | Get a finding routing rule by ID. |
| `c1i findings routing-rules create --body-file <file>` | Create a finding routing rule from a JSON object. |
| `c1i findings routing-rules update <rule-id> --body-file <file>` | Update a finding routing rule from a JSON object. |
| `c1i findings routing-rules delete <rule-id>` | Delete a finding routing rule. |
| `c1i findings transformation-rules list [--limit <n>]` | List finding transformation rules. Returns a single, fixed-size page; there's no `--page-size` or `--page-token`. |
| `c1i findings transformation-rules get <rule-id>` | Get a finding transformation rule by ID. |
| `c1i findings transformation-rules create --body-file <file>` | Create a finding transformation rule from a JSON object. |
| `c1i findings transformation-rules update <rule-id> --body-file <file>` | Update a transformation rule from a JSON object. |
| `c1i findings transformation-rules delete <rule-id>` | Delete a finding transformation rule. |
| [`c1i findings settings show`](#findings-settings-show) | Show finding detector settings. |
| `c1i findings settings update --body-file <file>` | Atomically update finding detector settings from a JSON object. |
| [`c1i findings settings edge show`](#findings-settings-edge-show) | Show which Edge finding categories and subcategories are detected. |
| [`c1i findings settings edge update`](#findings-settings-edge-update) | Enable or disable Edge finding categories from a JSON object. |
| `c1i findings audits search [--body-file <file>]` | Search finding audit events from an optional JSON object; without `--body-file`, it searches all accessible audit events. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| `c1i findings shadow-mcp-occurrences search --body-file <file>` | Search occurrences behind shadow MCP findings from a JSON object. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |

Output shapes differ by verb: `bulk-state` and `bulk-create-tasks` print `{"bulkActionId": ...}`, `settings update` prints `{"list":[...]}`, `create-task` prints `{"finding":{...},"taskId":...}` (so its IDs are `.taskId` and `.finding.id`), and deletes print `{}`. Every other mutation prints the finding or rule unwrapped like `get`, so its ID is `.id`.

### `findings search`

[Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--body-file string` | Search-request JSON object (file, or `-` for stdin): may contain `query`, `severities`, `states`, `findingTypes`, `appIds`, target IDs, `sourceKinds`, `refs`, `assigneeIdentityUserIds`, and the other public search filters. |

A missing `--body-file` searches all accessible findings. Paging belongs to `--page-size` and `--page-token`; a `pageToken` or `pageSize` in the file exits with code 2. `findings audits search` takes the same optional body and the same refusal.

`findings shadow-mcp-occurrences search` also refuses `pageSize`/`pageToken` in its body, but the body is required and must carry the finding's 27-character `findingId`; a body without it gets a `400`.

### `findings settings show`

Show the tenant's finding detector settings as returned. The raw object is not projected, so an explicit `--fields` is a usage error (exit `2`) rather than being ignored.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i findings settings show
```

### `findings settings edge show`

Show whether each Edge finding category (`list`) and subcategory (`subcategories`) is detected, as returned. Categories never configured read back their shipped default. The Edge finding type's own switch — `findings settings show` — still gates every category. As with `settings show`, the raw object is not projected, so an explicit `--fields` is a usage error.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i findings settings edge show
```

### `findings settings edge update`

Enable or disable detection for one or more Edge finding categories in a single write. The body names only what changes:

```json
{
  "settings": [
    {"category": "EDGE_FINDING_CATEGORY_DATA_VOLUME", "enabled": true}
  ],
  "subcategorySettings": [{"subcategory": "...", "enabled": false}]
}
```

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i findings settings edge update --body-file <file>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--body-file string` | JSON request object (file, or `-` for stdin). (Required.) |

A body without a non-empty `settings` or `subcategorySettings` exits with code 2 and nothing is sent. An unknown category is a `400`, which exits `2`. Prints the resulting settings as formatted JSON, like `findings settings edge show`.

### `findings create-task`

`--policy-id` is optional; C1 otherwise selects the app policy or the built-in Finding Review policy.

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--policy-id string` | Optional remediation policy ID. |

A referenced policy is looked up first: an unknown ID exits `4` and a deleted one exits `2` before anything is sent, and the credential needs policy read (else exit `3`), `--dry-run` included.

## Role mining

Manage role mining: analyses that cluster users into cohorts sharing similar entitlements, the suggestions those analyses produce, and the access profiles you can generate from a cohort.

Mutations and searches take JSON request objects through `--body-file` so profile filters, entitlement selections, and full configuration documents reach the API unchanged; pass `--body-file -` to read JSON from standard input. The fixed `list` commands (`suggestions list`, `runs list`, `custom-analysis list`) return a single, fixed-size page capped only by `--limit`.

Mutations print the API's response. `suggestions state` prints the suggestion, and `config update` the configuration, unwrapped like `config show`; the rest keep their own shape — `.runId` for `trigger`, `.id` for `custom-analysis trigger`, and `.accessProfileId` for `access-profiles create`. `--fields` is a usage error on those mutations.

`suggestions search --body-file` and `suggestions users --body-file` are required and refuse paging keys in the file (`pageSize`/`page_size`, `pageToken`/`page_token`); `suggestions users` also refuses `suggestionId`/`suggestion_id` unless it equals `<suggestion-id>`. Paging belongs to `--page-size` and `--page-token`. Any other `--body-file` in this family (`suggestions state`, `config update`, `custom-analysis trigger|evaluate`, `access-profiles create`) is a required JSON object.

| Command | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `c1i role-mining trigger` | Queue an organization-wide role mining analysis. |
| `c1i role-mining suggestions list [--limit <n>]` | List role mining suggestions. Returns a single, fixed-size page; use `suggestions search` for a paginated, filtered view. |
| `c1i role-mining suggestions search --body-file <file>` | Search role mining suggestions from a JSON object. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| `c1i role-mining suggestions get <suggestion-id>` | Get a role mining suggestion by ID. |
| `c1i role-mining suggestions state <suggestion-id> --body-file <file>` | Update a suggestion's state from a JSON object. |
| `c1i role-mining suggestions users <suggestion-id> --body-file <file>` | Search the users in a suggestion's cohort from a JSON object. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| `c1i role-mining config show` | Show role mining configuration. |
| `c1i role-mining config update --body-file <file>` | Replace role mining configuration from a full JSON configuration object. |
| `c1i role-mining runs list [--limit <n>]` | List role mining analysis runs. Returns a single, fixed-size page. |
| `c1i role-mining runs latest` | Get the latest role mining analysis run. |
| `c1i role-mining custom-analysis list [--limit <n>]` | List personal role mining custom analyses. Returns a single, fixed-size page. |
| `c1i role-mining custom-analysis latest` | Get the latest personal custom analysis. |
| `c1i role-mining custom-analysis get <analysis-id>` | Get a custom analysis result by ID. |
| `c1i role-mining custom-analysis trigger --body-file <file>` | Queue a personal custom analysis from a JSON object. |
| `c1i role-mining custom-analysis evaluate <analysis-id> --body-file <file>` | Evaluate an entitlement selection against an analysis from a JSON object. |
| `c1i role-mining access-profiles create --body-file <file>` | Create an access profile from a role mining cohort's JSON object. |

## Automations

List C1 automations, inspect, create, update, execute, or delete one, query the run history of their executions, and clear a tripped circuit breaker. `executions list` is the usual starting point for "did this automation run?" and "why did it fail?".

| Command | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| [`c1i automations list`](#automations-list) | List automations. |
| `c1i automations get <automation-id>` | Get a single automation. |
| [`c1i automations create`](#automations-create) | Create an automation. |
| [`c1i automations update`](#automations-update) | Update an automation's name, description, or enabled state, or any other field from a JSON object. |
| [`c1i automations delete`](#automations-delete) | Soft-delete an automation. |
| [`c1i automations execute`](#automations-execute) | Run an automation now, optionally waiting for the execution to end. |
| [`c1i automations executions list`](#automations-executions-list) | Stream automation execution history. |
| `c1i automations executions get <execution-id>` | Get one execution by ID. |
| `c1i automations executions terminate <execution-id>` | Terminate a running execution. |
| [`c1i automations cancel-pending`](#automations-cancel-pending) | Cancel the automation's queued executions. |
| [`c1i automations circuit-breaker clear`](#automations-circuit-breaker-clear) | Clear the automation's circuit breaker. |
| [`c1i automations circuit-breaker resolve-paused`](#automations-circuit-breaker-resolve-paused) | Run or cancel the executions paused by the breaker. |

### `automations list`

[Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--enabled-only` | Only emit automations that are currently enabled. |
| `--calls-function string` | Only emit automations that invoke the given function ID, in any step. |

### `automations create`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i automations create --display-name <name> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--display-name string` | Display name. Can't be blank: empty, whitespace, or invisible-only. (Required unless `--body-file`.) |
| `--description string` | Description. |
| `--enabled` | Create enabled; `--enabled=false` creates disabled. Omit to send neither, which leaves the server default (`false`). |
| `--steps-file string` | JSON array of `AutomationStep` objects, sent as `automationSteps` (file, or `-` for stdin). |
| `--triggers-file string` | JSON array of `AutomationTrigger` objects, sent as `triggers` (file, or `-` for stdin). |
| `--body-file string` | Full create-automation request body, verbatim (file, or `-` for stdin); mutually exclusive with every other flag. |

An automation with no triggers runs only through `automations execute`. `--body-file` covers what the flags don't: `circuitBreakerMax`/`circuitBreakerPeriod`, draft steps and triggers, `appId`, annotations, and context. Only one of `--body-file`, `--steps-file`, and `--triggers-file` can read stdin (`-`).

The API currently rejects an enabled creation with no steps with a `500` (`steps cannot be nil`): create it disabled, add steps, then enable it with `automations update --enabled`.

Prints the automation unwrapped like `get`, so its ID is `.id`. The response's `webhookCapabilityUrl` and `webhookHmacSecret` stay top-level siblings.

### `automations update`

Update an automation's display name, description, or enabled state. c1i builds the update mask from the flags you pass, so an explicit `--enabled=false` or an empty `--description` is a change, and an omitted flag is left alone. These flags leave the automation's steps and triggers unchanged; the server accepts `--enabled` on an automation with `isDraft` true and leaves `isDraft` as it was.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i automations update <automation-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--display-name string` | New display name. Can't be blank (exit `2`, nothing sent; the server answers an empty name with a `500`). |
| `--description string` | New description. Pass an empty value to clear it. |
| `--enabled` | Enable the automation, or disable it with `--enabled=false`. |
| `--body-file string` | Automation JSON object, sent verbatim (file, or `-` for stdin). Requires `--update-mask`, and can't be combined with the flags above. Must not contain `id` (or must equal `<automation-id>`). |
| `--update-mask string` | Comma-separated camelCase field paths to update. Required with `--body-file`; without `--body-file` it exits with code 2. |

To change anything else, such as triggers, steps, or draft fields, pass an automation object with `--body-file` and name the fields to change in `--update-mask`. Masking `triggers` replaces the published triggers and increments `currentVersion`; `isDraft` and `draftTriggers` stay as they were. An unknown camelCase path is a `400` (`invalid update_mask`) and a snake_case path a `400` (`FieldMask.paths contains invalid path`), both exit `2`.

The updated automation is returned as formatted JSON, unwrapped like `automations get`. `webhookCapabilityUrl` and `webhookHmacSecret` stay top-level fields.

**Example:**

```bash
c1i automations update <automation-id> --enabled=false
c1i automations update <automation-id> --body-file automation.json --update-mask triggers
```

<Note>
`automations create` and `automations update` refuse a step that uses `evaluateExpressions` or `createRevokeTasks`, in either spelling and whether it arrives through `--steps-file` or `--body-file` (including `draftAutomationSteps`). c1i names the step index, and for `createRevokeTasks` its replacement `createRevokeTasksV2`, and exits `2` before any request is sent, `--dry-run` included; a JSON `null` value means unset, not a use. The server now rejects both with `400` ("the evaluate expressions step is no longer supported"; "CreateRevokeTasks step is deprecated and no longer allowed. It is replaced by CreateRevokeTasksV2").
</Note>

### `automations delete`

Soft-delete an automation. The API's own list stops returning it, but `automations list` reads the search route the CLI pages with, and that index can still return the deleted automation with no deleted marker — so the row may stay listed here after the delete. `automations get` still returns it at exit `0`, with no field marking it deleted, and deleting it again exits `0` again. Prints `{}`; exit `0` means success.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i automations delete <automation-id>
```

### `automations execute`

Start an on-demand execution. Prints `{"executionId": "..."}`; read the execution with `automations executions get <execution-id>`, and stop it with `executions terminate <execution-id>`.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i automations execute <automation-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--context-file string` | JSON object sent as the execution's context — the values the automation's steps read (file, or `-` for stdin). |
| `--wait` | Poll the execution until it ends, or `--wait-timeout` elapses. |
| `--wait-timeout duration` | Maximum time to wait with `--wait` (default `4m0s`). |

With `--wait`, the execution ID is printed as soon as the execution is accepted, and progress goes to stderr. An execution that ends in `AUTOMATION_EXECUTION_STATE_ERROR` or `_TERMINATE`, or is paused by the circuit breaker, exits `1` with an error naming the state — the execution was started, so the error carries `applied: true`. A timeout also exits `1`, and the execution may still be running. `--dry-run` never polls.

**Example:**

```bash
EXEC_ID=$(c1i automations execute "$AUTOMATION" | jq -er .executionId)
c1i automations execute "$AUTOMATION" --wait --wait-timeout 10m
```

### `automations executions list`

[Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--state string` | Filter by execution state: `done`, `error`, `pending`, `creating`, `waiting`, `terminate`, or any `AUTOMATION_EXECUTION_STATE_*` value. |
| `--template-id string` | Filter by automation template (the parent automation's ID). |

c1i applies `--state` and `--template-id` client-side after fetching each page, because the executions endpoint doesn't support server-side filtering. A narrow filter still scans every page the server returns, so combine it with `--limit` to bound the work.

**Example:**

```bash
c1i automations executions list --state error --limit 20
```

### `automations executions get` and `automations executions terminate`

`executions get` takes the `id` of an `executions list` row, or the `executionId` that `automations execute` prints, and unwraps the execution to the top level: `.state` is its `AUTOMATION_EXECUTION_STATE_*` value and `.automationTemplateId` the automation it belongs to.

`executions terminate` stops a running execution. The API describes termination as asynchronous, so confirm it with `executions get`. It prints `{}`; exit `0` is the success signal.

### `automations cancel-pending`

Cancel the automation's queued executions that have not started. The API describes executions already running, and ones started manually, as unaffected; stop a running one with `executions terminate`.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i automations cancel-pending <automation-id> [--reason <text>]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--reason string` | Reason recorded in the system log. |

Prints `{"bulkActionId": "..."}`. The API describes cancellation as asynchronous, so exit `0` means accepted, not finished, and c1i has no command to read a bulk action. A tenant without the feature answers `400 cancelling queued automation runs is not enabled for this tenant` (exit `2`).

### `automations circuit-breaker clear`

Clear the circuit breaker on an automation it disabled. Without `--decision`, only the breaker is cleared and executions already paused stay paused; `--decision run` or `cancel` also resolves them in the same request.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i automations circuit-breaker clear <automation-id> [--decision <run|cancel>] \
  [--reason <text>]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--decision string` | Also resolve paused executions: `run`, `cancel`, or the full `PAUSED_EXECUTION_DECISION_*` name of one. Omit to clear the breaker only. |
| `--reason string` | Reason recorded with the decision. |

Prints `{"bulkActionId": "..."}`; without `--decision` the ID is empty, and with one an ID came back even when nothing was paused. The API describes the resolution as asynchronous, so exit `0` means accepted, not finished.

### `automations circuit-breaker resolve-paused`

Run or cancel the executions paused while the automation's circuit breaker was tripped. `--decision` is required.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i automations circuit-breaker resolve-paused <automation-id> \
  --decision <run|cancel> [--reason <text>]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--decision string` | What to do with the paused executions: `run`, `cancel`, or the full `PAUSED_EXECUTION_DECISION_*` name of one. (Required.) |
| `--reason string` | Reason recorded with the decision. |

Prints `{"bulkActionId": "..."}`; the response also carries the deprecated `pausedCount` and `erroredCount`, which were always `0` in the runs checked even when executions were resolved.

## Functions

Manage C1 functions: the serverless TypeScript modules that run inside C1's sandbox, invoked as automation steps, from the UI, or through the API. A function's code lives in commits; the published commit is the one that runs.

| Command | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| [`c1i functions list`](#functions-list) | List C1 functions. |
| [`c1i functions get`](#functions-get) | Get a single function's metadata. |
| [`c1i functions create`](#functions-create) | Create a function, uploading local source as its first commit. |
| [`c1i functions update`](#functions-update) | Update metadata or push a commit, and publish a commit. |
| [`c1i functions delete`](#functions-delete) | Soft-delete a function. |
| [`c1i functions source`](#functions-source) | Fetch a function's source files. |
| [`c1i functions commits`](#functions-commits) | List a function's commit history. |
| [`c1i functions usage`](#functions-usage) | Find the automation steps that call a function. |
| [`c1i functions invoke`](#functions-invoke) | Invoke a function, optionally waiting for the result. |
| [`c1i functions invocations list`](#functions-invocations-list) | List a function's invocations. |
| [`c1i functions invocations get`](#functions-invocations-get) | Read one invocation, with its input, output, and error. |

A create or an update that pushes files leaves the new commit unpublished: `functions invoke` runs the published commit by default, so publish with [`functions update --publish-commit`](#functions-update). `list`, `commits`, `usage`, and `invocations list` output NDJSON; everything else outputs the object unwrapped, so the ID is `.id`.

### `functions list`

[Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--published-only` | Only include functions with a published commit (excludes drafts). |
| `--draft-only` | Only include functions that are drafts or have never been published. |

### `functions get`

Get a single function's metadata as pretty JSON. The function itself is the document, not the API's wrapper.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i functions get <function-id>
```

### `functions create`

Create a TypeScript function of type `FUNCTION_TYPE_ANY`, the type the Functions UI lists. `--display-name` is required.

Each `--file` uploads one local file as the initial commit, named by its base name: `--file ./src/main.ts` becomes `main.ts`, the entry file. The API's own description says `main.ts` must export a default async handler that returns a JSON object. Two files with the same base name exit `2`. Without `--file` the function is created with no commit.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i functions create --display-name <name> [--file <path>...] [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--display-name string` | Display name. (Required.) |
| `--description string` | Description. An empty value is sent as an empty description. |
| `--file stringArray` | Local source file for the initial commit, uploaded under its base name (repeatable; `main.ts` is the entry file). |
| `--commit-message string` | Message of the initial commit, which the API requires (default `Initial commit`). |

The new function is unpublished. To publish the initial commit, pass its ID (`.commit.id` in this command's output) to [`functions update --publish-commit`](#functions-update).

Output is the function, unwrapped like `functions get`: the ID is `.id`, and the initial commit is the sibling `.commit` (`null` without `--file`). Honors `--dry-run`.

### `functions update`

Update a function. The update mask is derived from the flags you pass, so an omitted flag is left alone.

`--file` pushes a new commit — the function's new head — from local files named by base name as in `functions create`; it does not publish it. Publishing is a separate call: pass the new commit's ID (`.commit.id` in this command's output) to `--publish-commit`. `--commit-message` names the pushed commit; without it the server writes `Updated function content`.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i functions update <function-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--display-name string` | New display name. Omit to leave unchanged. |
| `--description string` | New description. An empty value clears it; omit to leave unchanged. |
| `--file stringArray` | Local source file for a new commit, uploaded under its base name (repeatable). Not published. |
| `--commit-message string` | Message of the pushed commit. Omit for the server's default. |
| `--publish-commit string` | Commit ID to publish as the function's runnable version. Omit to leave unchanged. |
| `--body-file string` | Function JSON object, sent with the ID set (file, or `-` for stdin). Requires `--update-mask`, and can't be combined with `--display-name`, `--description`, or `--publish-commit`; `--file` still applies. |
| `--update-mask string` | Comma-separated camelCase field paths. Required with `--body-file`; passing it without `--body-file` exits `2`. Otherwise the mask is derived from the flags you pass. |

The server validates the function's display name on every update, even when it isn't changing, so without `--display-name` c1i reads the function first and resends its current name; that read also runs on `--dry-run`.

For any other field — `networkConfig`, `outboundNetworkAllowlist`, `provisionedConcurrency`, `scopedRoleIds`, `secret`, and so on — pass a function object with `--body-file` and name the fields in `--update-mask`. The object is sent with `id` set to `<function-id>`. Mask paths are camelCase: `published_commit_id` is a `400 invalid path`, and a mask naming `displayName` with no `displayName` in the object exits `2` (c1i would otherwise resend the current name, a silent no-op). Secret values come back as `""`.

Output is the function, unwrapped like `functions get`, with the pushed commit as the sibling `.commit` (`null` without `--file`). Honors `--dry-run`.

### `functions delete`

Soft-delete a function. It leaves `functions list` while `functions get` still returns it with `deletedAt` set; deleting it again exits `4`. The API refuses a function that a hook or workflow template still references until that reference is removed or retargeted.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i functions delete <function-id>
```

Prints `{}`; exit `0` means success. Honors `--dry-run`.

### `functions source`

Fetch the TypeScript source for a function. Without `--commit`, c1i resolves the function's published commit, falling back to its head — the latest draft — if nothing is published. The API returns source files base64-encoded under a `files` map; c1i decodes them and prints each file as text, separated by a delimiter line.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i functions source <function-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--commit string` | Commit ID to fetch (default: the function's published commit, falling back to head). |
| `--out-dir string` | Write files to this directory instead of stdout, one file per source file, named exactly as the function has them (usually `main.ts` and `main.test.ts`). |

With `--out-dir`, c1i writes files with mode `0600` and creates the directory (or tightens it, never widens it, stripping special bits) to at most `0700`, because fetched source can inline credentials.

### `functions commits`

List a function's commit history. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

Rows are `id`, `function_id`, `author`, `message`, and `created_at`.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i functions commits <function-id> [flags]
```

### `functions usage`

Scan the tenant's automations and emit one NDJSON row per automation step that calls the function. Use it to check whether a function is still used before deleting a draft, or to find an example automation that exercises a published function.

c1i reads `POST /api/v1/search/automations` and inspects `automationSteps[].callFunction`. Row fields are `automation_id`, `automation_name`, `step_name`, `enabled`, `last_executed_at`, and `args`. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i functions usage <function-id> [flags]
```

### `functions invoke`

Start an invocation of a function and print the API's response, whose `.invocationId` names it. The run is asynchronous: read its status, output, and error with [`functions invocations get`](#functions-invocations-get).

Without `--commit-id`, c1i reads the function and runs its published commit; a function with nothing published exits `2` (sent without a commit ID, the API answers a `400 commit_id is required`). A `--commit-id` is checked first: one the function doesn't have exits `4`, since the server would otherwise accept it and leave the invocation running. Both reads also run on `--dry-run`.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i functions invoke <function-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--commit-id string` | Commit to run (default: the function's published commit). |
| `--input string` | Handler input as a JSON object (for example `'{"a":1}'`). |
| `--input-file string` | File holding the handler input JSON object (`-` for stdin). |
| `--wait` | Block and poll the invocation until it succeeds (exit `0`) or fails (exit `1`), or `--wait-timeout` elapses. |
| `--wait-timeout duration` | Maximum time to wait with `--wait` (for example `30s` or `5m`); reaching it exits `1` (default `5m0s`). |

`--input` and `--input-file` take the handler's input and must be a JSON object; omit both to send none, and the handler then receives `{}`.

With `--wait`, c1i polls the invocation every 2 seconds after printing the response. Any `--wait` failure exits `1` with an error saying the invoke "was applied", which means only that the invocation was started: a run that failed is the error naming `ended in FUNCTION_INVOCATION_STATUS_ERROR` (or `CANCELLED`) and the function's error, while a timeout says it timed out and is not necessarily a failure. The run's output is in `functions invocations get`.

### `functions invocations list`

List a function's invocations, newest first, one NDJSON row each: `id`, `function_id`, `commit_id`, `status`, `error`, `created_at`, and `updated_at`. Input and output are left out of the rows; read them with `functions invocations get`.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i functions invocations list <function-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--limit int` | Cap the total number of results; `0` is unlimited. |
| `--page-size int` | Rows to request per API page (max `100`; default `50`). |
| `--page-token string` | Pagination cursor. A non-empty value fetches exactly one page. |

A busy function can have tens of thousands of invocations and this command pages through all of them, so pass `--limit` for the most recent N. The search response carries each invocation's full input and output and the API asks for a small page size; use `--page-size 10` when payloads may be large.

### `functions invocations get`

Get one invocation, with its input, output, and error, as pretty JSON.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i functions invocations get <invocation-id> --function-id <function-id>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--function-id string` | Function the invocation belongs to. (Required.) |

## Export

Export bulk data from C1. `export events` dumps the audit log to stdout as an NDJSON stream, for archiving or shipping to another system. `export exporters` manages system log exporters, which write the same events continuously to a datasource without a CLI run per batch.

| Command | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| [`c1i export events`](#export-events) | Dump system log (audit) events as NDJSON. |
| [`c1i export exporters list`](#export-exporters-list) | List system log exporters. |
| [`c1i export exporters search`](#export-exporters-search) | Search exporters by text or ID. |
| [`c1i export exporters get`](#export-exporters-get) | Get a single exporter. |
| [`c1i export exporters create`](#export-exporters-create) | Create an exporter that writes to an existing datasource. |
| [`c1i export exporters update`](#export-exporters-update) | Change an exporter's name, prefix, or format. |
| [`c1i export exporters delete`](#export-exporters-delete) | Soft-delete an exporter. |
| [`c1i export exporters events`](#export-exporters-events) | List the events belonging to one exporter. |

### `export events`

Export C1 system log events, in OCSF format, as an NDJSON stream with one event per line. Redirect the output to a file to archive events or ship them to an external system. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--since string` | Only events at or after this RFC 3339 time. |
| `--until string` | Only events before this RFC 3339 time. |
| `--since-event-uid string` | Resume after this event UID (incremental sync). |
| `--sort string` | Chronological order: `asc` (oldest first), `desc`, or the full `SORT_DIRECTION_*` name of one (default `asc`). |

Both timestamps must sit within about one year of now: the server rejects a future `--until`, or a `--since` older than roughly 365 days, with a `400 value must be less than now within 8761h6m40s` (exit `2`).

**Examples:**

```bash
# Everything, oldest first, to a file
c1i export events > audit.ndjson

# A specific time window
c1i export events --since 2026-07-01T00:00:00Z --until 2026-07-08T00:00:00Z

# Incremental sync: resume after the last event you already stored
c1i export events --since-event-uid <last-event-uid>
```

### `export exporters list`

List system log exporters, one row per exporter as the API returns it (`exportId`, `displayName`, `state`, `datasource`, and so on). A deleted exporter isn't listed, while [`exporters get`](#export-exporters-get) still returns it with `deletedAt` set. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i export exporters list [flags]
```

### `export exporters search`

Search system log exporters by free text with `--query` and by ID with `--export-id`. Rows are shaped like `exporters list`. An unknown `--export-id` matches nothing; it doesn't widen the result.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i export exporters search [--query <text>] [--export-id <id>] [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--query string` | Free-text search. |
| `--export-id stringArray` | Match this exporter ID (repeatable). |

There is no `--name` filter: the API's `displayName` search field was observed to return an exporter whatever the name, so c1i doesn't expose it. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

### `export exporters get`

Get one exporter by ID. A deleted exporter is still returned, with `deletedAt` set. Prints the exporter unwrapped, so the ID is `.exportId`.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i export exporters get <export-id>
```

### `export exporters create`

Create an exporter that writes audit events to an existing datasource, under `--prefix`, in `--format`.

`--datasource-id` must name a datasource that already exists. No datasource API is exposed to check it first, so pass the `.datasource.datasourceId` of an existing exporter — from `exporters list` or `exporters get`. The API answers an unknown one with a `400 datasource not found`, which c1i reports as an unknown reference (exit `4`).

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i export exporters create --display-name <name> \
  --datasource-id <datasource-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--display-name string` | Display name for the exporter. Can't be blank. (Required.) |
| `--datasource-id string` | Existing datasource to write to. (Required.) |
| `--prefix string` | Path prefix under the datasource. Omit for none; can't be blank. |
| `--format string` | Output format: `zstd`, `gzip`, or the full `EXPORT_FORMAT_*` name of one. Omitted, it stays `EXPORT_FORMAT_UNSPECIFIED`, which the API accepted. |

<Warning>
The new exporter starts in state `EXPORT_STATE_EXPORTING`: it begins writing to the datasource immediately, so choose `--prefix` deliberately.
</Warning>

Prints the exporter unwrapped like `exporters get`, so read `.exportId`. Honors `--dry-run`.

### `export exporters update`

Change an exporter's `--display-name`, or its datasource `--prefix` or `--format`. The mask is derived from the flags you pass, so an omitted flag is left alone. The datasource ID itself can't be changed here.

The API refuses an update whose exporter has no datasource (`value is required`), so c1i first reads the exporter and sends its current datasource back with only your flags changed; `--dry-run` does the read too. The read and the write aren't atomic.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i export exporters update <export-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--display-name string` | New display name. Omit to leave unchanged; can't be blank. |
| `--prefix string` | New path prefix under the datasource. Omit to leave unchanged; can't be blank. |
| `--format string` | New output format: `zstd`, `gzip`, or the full `EXPORT_FORMAT_*` name of one. Omit to leave unchanged. |

`update` also succeeds on a soft-deleted exporter — it changes it, exits `0`, and keeps `deletedAt` — so check `deletedAt` rather than reading exit `0` as live. With the mask set, the API changed only the masked fields; a differing unmasked name, prefix, or format in the same request was ignored in the run checked.

Prints the exporter unwrapped like `exporters get` (ID: `.exportId`).

### `export exporters delete`

Delete an exporter by ID. The delete is soft: `exporters get` still returns it with `deletedAt` set, and `exporters list` omits it. A repeat delete exits `0` and stamps `deletedAt` again.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i export exporters delete <export-id>
```

Prints `{}`; exit `0` means success. Honors `--dry-run`.

### `export exporters events`

List the OCSF events belonging to one system log exporter, one JSON event per line, auto-paginating. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

c1i reads the exporter first, so an unknown ID exits `4` — the API answers a well-formed unknown ID with an empty `200`. A soft-deleted exporter's events are still listed; check `deletedAt` with `exporters get` if you need to know whether the exporter is live.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i export exporters events <export-id> [flags]
```

## Upgrade

Check for and install a newer c1i release. The command is aliased as `update`.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i upgrade [--channel <stable|latest|preview>] \
  [--check] [-y]
```

c1i follows a release channel from the C1 distribution center (`dist.conductorone.com`); see [Upgrade c1i](/product/cli/c1i#upgrade-c1i). Before it replaces a standalone binary, it verifies the release manifest's Sigstore signature (signed by the release workflow run for that c1i tag) and the download's SHA-256 from that manifest. For a Homebrew, `go install`, or container install, `upgrade` prints that method's upgrade command and exits 0 without replacing anything.

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--channel string` | Release channel: `stable`, `latest`, or `preview` (default `stable`). |
| `--check` | Print a JSON report of whether a newer release is available; change nothing. |
| `-y`, `--yes` | Skip the confirmation prompt. Required when stdin is not a terminal. |

`--check` prints one JSON object with these keys:

| Key | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `current` | The version you run. |
| `latest` | The newest version on the channel. |
| `channel` | The release channel checked. |
| `update_available` | `true` when a newer release exists. |
| `install_method` | How c1i was installed: `standalone`, `homebrew`, `go-install`, `container`, `system`, or `windows`. |
| `upgrade_command` | The command that upgrades this install. Present only for a Homebrew, `go install`, or container install. |

Windows and system installs print a hint but no `upgrade_command`.

With the global `--dry-run` flag, `upgrade` fetches and verifies the release (index, manifest, signature, and TUF root) and checks that the install directory is writable. It prints one JSON object on stdout: `dryRun`, `requests` for the download it would make (`[]` when no download is needed), and an `upgrade` object with the `--check` fields; for a standalone update it also includes `asset_url`, `sha256`, and `destination`. It takes no lock, doesn't prompt, and installs nothing. `--fields` is a usage error unless you pass `--check`.

`upgrade` treats the distribution center as a system beyond C1. A distribution center `404`, `429`, or `5xx` exits `4`, `5`, or `6`, and a same-path redirect loop exits `6`. Any other `4xx`, a refused redirect, a non-JSON `200`, or a TUF, signature, or checksum failure exits `8`.

## Service principals

Manage service principals — tenant-owned non-human identities. The group is aliased as `service-principal` and `sp`, and its `credentials` subgroup as `credential` and `creds`.

A service principal is an identity; a client credential on it is what a caller authenticates with (a client ID and secret). A binding lets another subject — a function, SSO application, AuthZEN server, or edge — act as the principal. The bindings surface is a draft API and may change. A trust instead lets an external workload's own JWT act as the principal, when the token comes from a provider registered with [`workload-federation providers`](#workload-federation) and its claims satisfy the trust's CEL condition; see [Workload federation](/product/admin/service-principals/workload-federation).

| Command | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| [`c1i service-principals list`](#service-principals) | List service principals. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| `c1i service-principals get <sp-id>` | Get a single service principal by ID. |
| `c1i service-principals create --display-name <name>` | Create a service principal. |
| `c1i service-principals update <sp-id> --display-name <name>` | Update a service principal's display name. |
| `c1i service-principals delete <sp-id>` | Delete a service principal, its client credentials, and its trusts. Prints `{}`. |
| `c1i service-principals credentials list <sp-id>` | List a service principal's client credentials. The secret itself is never part of a row — it's returned only once, at creation. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| `c1i service-principals credentials get <credential-id> --service-principal-id <sp-id>` | Get a single client credential. |
| [`c1i service-principals credentials create`](#service-principals-credentials-create) | Create a client credential (the secret is shown once). |
| `c1i service-principals credentials update <credential-id> --service-principal-id <sp-id> --display-name <name>` | Update a client credential's display name. |
| `c1i service-principals credentials revoke <credential-id> --service-principal-id <sp-id>` | Revoke (delete) a client credential. Prints `{}`. |
| [`c1i service-principals bindings add`](#service-principals-bindings-add) | Bind a subject to a service principal. |
| `c1i service-principals bindings list [subject flags]` | List the service principals a subject is bound to. Takes the same subject flags as `bindings add`. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| `c1i service-principals bindings delete --service-principal-id <sp-id> [subject flags]` | Remove a subject's binding to a service principal. Takes the same flags as `bindings add`. Idempotent; prints `{}`. |
| `c1i service-principals trusts list <sp-id>` | List a service principal's workload federation trusts. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| [`c1i service-principals trusts search`](#service-principals-trusts-search) | Search trusts across all service principals. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| `c1i service-principals trusts get <trust-id> --service-principal-id <sp-id>` | Get a single trust. |
| [`c1i service-principals trusts create`](#service-principals-trusts-create) | Create a trust from a provider and a CEL condition. |
| [`c1i service-principals trusts update`](#service-principals-trusts-update) | Update a trust; list flags replace the whole list. |
| `c1i service-principals trusts delete <trust-id> --service-principal-id <sp-id>` | Delete a trust. Prints `{}`. |
| [`c1i service-principals trusts test-token`](#service-principals-trusts-test-token) | Check a workload JWT against a trust without issuing a token. |

Deletes and revocations are soft, but what `get` shows differs by object. A deleted service principal is reflected immediately: `get` exits `4` and `list` omits it, while `users get <sp-id>` shows `"status": "DELETED"` and `deletedAt`. A revoked credential is still returned by `credentials get`, unchanged and at exit `0`, with no field marking it revoked; only `credentials list` omits it. A deleted trust leaves `trusts list` and `trusts search` at once, while `trusts get` still returns it, with no field marking it deleted — and deleting the service principal deletes its trusts the same way. Repeating the principal delete or the trust delete exits `0`.

A trust is addressed by its client ID, as `trusts list` and `trusts search` print it (for example `word-word-12345@tenant.example.com/wfe`). `<trust-id>` takes either that full ID or the part before `@`; c1i sends only the part before `@`, the only form the API routes, and the rest is not checked, while the trust is still looked up under `--service-principal-id`. A row prints an absent `scoped_role_ids`, `allow_source_cidrs` or `passthrough_claims` as `null`, not `[]`.

### `service-principals credentials create`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i service-principals credentials create <sp-id> --display-name <name> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--display-name string` | Display name for the new credential. Cannot be blank (empty, whitespace or invisible-only). (Required.) |
| `--scoped-role stringArray` | Restrict the credential to a role ID from [`c1i roles list`](#roles) (repeatable). |
| `--allow-cidr stringArray` | Restrict the credential to a source CIDR (repeatable). |
| `--expires string` | Time until the credential expires, as a whole-number Go duration (for example `720h`) or a protobuf duration (`3600s`); a sign, decimal, or leading zero exits with code 2. The server accepts the range `(0s, 4320h]` (up to 180 days). |
| `--require-dpop` | Require DPoP proof-of-possession for token exchange. |
