c1i prints the credential unwrapped, with the secret as a sibling field: read `.id`, `.clientId`, and `.clientSecret`. The secret appears only in this output, so capture it now.

### `service-principals bindings add`

Idempotent; prints `{}`.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i service-principals bindings add --service-principal-id <sp-id> \
  [subject flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--service-principal-id string` | Service principal ID. (Required.) |
| `--function-id string` | Subject: function ID (the function acts as this service principal). |
| `--sso-application-id string` | Subject: SSO application ID. Requires `--sso-app-id`. |
| `--sso-app-id string` | App ID owning `--sso-application-id`. |
| `--authzen-server-id string` | Subject: AuthZEN server ID. Requires `--authzen-app-id`. |
| `--authzen-app-id string` | App ID owning `--authzen-server-id`. |
| `--edge-id string` | Subject: edge ID. Requires `--edge-app-id`. |
| `--edge-app-id string` | App ID owning `--edge-id`. |

Exactly one subject must be given, and an app-scoped subject (`--sso-application-id`, `--authzen-server-id`, `--edge-id`) requires its paired `--*-app-id` flag.

### `service-principals trusts create`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i service-principals trusts create <sp-id> --provider-id <provider-id> \
  --display-name <name> --condition <cel> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--provider-id string` | Provider whose tokens the trust accepts, from [`workload-federation providers list`](#workload-federation-providers-list). Fixed after creation. (Required.) |
| `--display-name string` | Display name. Cannot be blank (empty, whitespace or invisible-only). (Required.) |
| `--condition string` | CEL expression over the token's claims; must return a bool, for example `claims.sub == "repo:acme/infra:ref:refs/heads/main"`. (Required.) |
| `--description string` | Description. |
| `--scoped-role stringArray` | Narrow the trust to a role ID from [`c1i roles list`](#roles) (repeatable). |
| `--allow-cidr stringArray` | Only accept token exchanges from this source CIDR (repeatable). |
| `--passthrough-claim stringArray` | Copy this string claim from the workload token into the issued token's `c1wfc` claim (repeatable). |

The API compiles `--condition` and refuses an invalid one (exit `2`); evaluate it against sample claims first with [`c1i workload-federation test-cel`](#workload-federation-test-cel). A new trust is enabled.

c1i looks up `<sp-id>` and each `--scoped-role` before sending, since the API stores a trust for an unknown id: an unknown one exits `4`, `--dry-run` included. The API answers a human user's id with a `500` ("user ... is not a service principal", exit `6`). An unknown `--provider-id` exits `4`.

Prints the trust as pretty JSON, unwrapped like `trusts get`; its client ID is `.clientId`.

### `service-principals trusts update`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i service-principals trusts update <trust-id> --service-principal-id <sp-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--service-principal-id string` | Service principal that owns the trust. (Required.) |
| `--display-name string` | New display name; cannot be blank (omit to leave unchanged). |
| `--description string` | New description; `""` is sent as an empty value (omit to leave unchanged). |
| `--condition string` | New CEL condition (omit to leave unchanged). |
| `--disabled` | Disable the trust (`--disabled=false` re-enables it, omit to leave unchanged). |
| `--scoped-role stringArray` | Replace the scoped role IDs (repeatable). |
| `--allow-cidr stringArray` | Replace the source CIDR allowlist (repeatable). |
| `--passthrough-claim stringArray` | Replace the passthrough claim names (repeatable). |
| `--body-file string` | Trust JSON object (file, or `-` for stdin); requires `--update-mask`. Mutually exclusive with the field flags, and must not set a different `clientId`: exit `2`. |
| `--update-mask string` | Comma-separated field paths: required with `--body-file`, rejected without it, derived otherwise. |

The update mask is derived from the flags you pass, and the provider is fixed at creation. `--scoped-role`, `--allow-cidr` and `--passthrough-claim` replace the whole list. To empty a list, pass a trust object via `--body-file` with an explicit `--update-mask` — for example `{"scopedRoleIds":[]}` with `--update-mask scopedRoleIds`. A mask that names a list the object leaves out, or sets to `null`, exits `2` with nothing sent: the API would clear it, and an empty `scopedRoleIds` gives the trust all of the principal's roles.

Each scoped role ID, from `--scoped-role` or from a `--body-file`'s `scopedRoleIds`/`scoped_role_ids`, is looked up first, since the API stores an unknown role ID: an unknown one exits `4` before anything is sent, `--dry-run` included. A trust that was deleted still accepts an update.

### `service-principals trusts search`

[Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--service-principal-id string` | Only trusts of this service principal. |
| `--provider-id string` | Only trusts that reference this provider. |
| `--query string` | Full-text match on display name and description, as the API documents it. |

Filters combine, and with none every trust is listed. An unknown `--service-principal-id` or `--provider-id` matches nothing and exits `0`.

### `service-principals trusts test-token`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i service-principals trusts test-token <trust-id> --service-principal-id <sp-id> \
  --subject-token-file <path|->
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--service-principal-id string` | Service principal that owns the trust. (Required.) |
| `--subject-token-file string` | File holding the workload JWT (`-` for stdin). (Required.) |
| `--source-ip string` | Source IP (optionally with a CIDR prefix) to check against the trust's allowlist, instead of the caller's. |

Checks a workload JWT against a trust, step by step, without issuing an access token, and changes nothing. The JWT is read from the file only, never from the command line, and surrounding whitespace is trimmed.

The API's report always prints: `overallResult`, `decodedClaimsJson`, and one object per step (`jwtDecode`, `issuerMatch`, `signatureValidation`, ...), each with `passed`, `skipped` and `detail`. Then the command exits `0` when `overallResult` is true, and `1` naming the failed steps when it is false. The API answers a disabled trust, a disabled provider or a deleted trust with a `500` ("trust is disabled", "provider is disabled", "trust is deleted"; exit `6`).

<Callout icon="globe" color="#c937ae" iconType="regular">These examples use `conductor.one`. If your organization is on the EU data residency instance, substitute `c1eu.ai` in URLs, client IDs, and hostnames.</Callout>

## Workload federation

Manage workload federation: an external workload — a CI job, a SPIFFE workload — exchanges its own JWT for a C1 token that acts as a service principal, so no client secret is stored anywhere. The group is aliased as `wf`.

A provider registers an external JWT issuer for the tenant. A trust, created per service principal with [`service-principals trusts`](#service-principals), names a provider and a CEL condition over the token's claims; the API documents a trust's effective permissions as `min(service principal roles, trust scoped roles)`. See [Workload federation](/product/admin/service-principals/workload-federation) for the product view, and [`test-cel`](#workload-federation-test-cel) to try a condition before you create a trust.

| Command | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| [`c1i workload-federation providers list`](#workload-federation-providers-list) | List the tenant's providers. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| [`c1i workload-federation providers get`](#workload-federation-providers-get) | Get a single provider. |
| [`c1i workload-federation providers create`](#workload-federation-providers-create) | Register an external JWT issuer. |
| [`c1i workload-federation providers update`](#workload-federation-providers-update) | Update a provider's name, description, `--disabled`, or SPIFFE bundle endpoint. |
| [`c1i workload-federation providers delete`](#workload-federation-providers-delete) | Delete a provider, once no trust references it. |
| [`c1i workload-federation test-cel`](#workload-federation-test-cel) | Evaluate a trust condition against sample claims. |

Mutations print the provider as pretty JSON, unwrapped like `get`, so read the ID from `.id`; `delete` prints `{}`.

### `workload-federation providers list`

[Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). Prints NDJSON. Rows carry `id`, `display_name`, `description`, `issuer_url`, `well_known_provider`, `disabled`, `created_at` and `updated_at`, plus `bundle_endpoint_url` on a SPIFFE provider. A deleted provider leaves the list at once.

### `workload-federation providers get`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i workload-federation providers get <provider-id>
```

Prints the provider unwrapped. A deleted provider is still returned, with no field marking it deleted.

### `workload-federation providers create`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i workload-federation providers create --display-name <name> \
  --well-known-provider <kind> --issuer-url <url> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--display-name string` | Display name. Cannot be blank (empty, whitespace or invisible-only). (Required.) |
| `--well-known-provider string` | Provider kind: `custom`, `github-actions`, `gitlab-ci`, `hcp-terraform`, `aws-iam-outbound`, `spiffe`, or the full `WELL_KNOWN_WORKLOAD_PROVIDER_*` name of one. (Required.) |
| `--issuer-url string` | Issuer: an `https://` OIDC issuer, or a `spiffe://` trust domain for `--well-known-provider spiffe`. (Required.) |
| `--bundle-endpoint-url string` | HTTPS JWKS URL of the SPIFFE trust domain's signing keys. `spiffe` only, and required there; any other kind refuses it (exit `2`, nothing sent). |
| `--description string` | Description. |

A `spiffe` provider takes a `spiffe://` trust-domain issuer and requires `--bundle-endpoint-url`. Every other kind takes an `https://` issuer, which the API checks by OIDC discovery. The API refuses an issuer it cannot reach ("OIDC discovery at ... is unreachable or invalid"), an issuer that does not fit a named kind (`github-actions` takes only `https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com`), and an issuer already registered in the tenant (`409`, exit `2`). The kind and issuer are fixed after creation, and a new provider is enabled.

**Example:**

```bash
c1i workload-federation providers create --display-name gha \
  --well-known-provider github-actions \
  --issuer-url https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com
```

### `workload-federation providers update`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i workload-federation providers update <provider-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--display-name string` | New display name; cannot be blank (omit to leave unchanged). |
| `--description string` | New description; `""` is sent as an empty value (omit to leave unchanged). |
| `--disabled` | Disable the provider (`--disabled=false` re-enables it, omit to leave unchanged). |
| `--bundle-endpoint-url string` | New JWKS URL for a SPIFFE provider (omit to leave unchanged). |

The update mask is derived from the flags you pass, so an omitted flag is left alone, and the kind and issuer are fixed. `--disabled` makes the provider reject every token exchange. `--bundle-endpoint-url` is for SPIFFE providers: the API re-checks it by fetching its JWKS and refuses one it cannot fetch (exit `2`). On any other provider the API still fetches it (an unfetchable URL is a `400`, exit `2`) and then discards it. A deleted provider still accepts an update (`200`) and stays out of `providers list`.

### `workload-federation providers delete`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i workload-federation providers delete <provider-id>
```

Honors `--dry-run` and prints `{}`; deleting it again also answers `{}`. The API refuses while a trust references the provider ("cannot delete provider: an active trust references this provider; delete trusts first", exit `2`); find them with `c1i service-principals trusts search --provider-id <provider-id>`, which can lag a just-created trust by about a second. The delete is soft: the provider leaves `providers list` at once, but `providers get` still returns it, with no field marking it deleted, and `providers update` still changes it.

### `workload-federation test-cel`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i workload-federation test-cel --expression <cel> \
  (--claims <json> | --claims-file <path|->)
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--expression string` | CEL expression over claims; must return a bool. (Required.) |
| `--claims string` | Claims as a JSON object (or a JWT). |
| `--claims-file string` | File holding the claims (`-` for stdin). |

Evaluates an expression against sample claims, as a trust's `--condition` would be, without a token, provider or trust; it changes nothing. The API also accepts a signed JWT and evaluates its claims — in the runs checked it did not verify the signature, and it refused `"alg": "none"`. Pass a JWT with `--claims-file`, not `--claims`, to keep it off the command line.

The API's answer always prints, with `matched`, `error` and `expression`:

| Exit | Meaning |
| :--- | :--- |
| `0` | The expression matched. |
| `1` | The expression evaluated to false. |
| `2` | The API reported an error: a syntax error, a non-bool result (such as `expression must return bool, got int`), or claims that are neither JSON nor a JWT. |

**Example:**

```bash
c1i workload-federation test-cel --expression 'claims.sub == "x"' --claims '{"sub":"x"}'
```

## XAA

Manage an app's cross-app access (XAA) objects: resource servers (the third-party authorization servers C1 mints grants for), their OAuth scopes, access profiles (requestable bundles of one resource server's scopes), the scopes bound to each profile, and each resource server's client audience mappings.

Resource servers, scopes and access profiles take `--app-id`; client audience mappings take `--resource-server-id` instead, because their routes carry no app. The tenant-wide switch, default grant lifetime and ID-token lifetime are in `c1i settings cross-app-access`. `xaa access-profiles` is not the request catalog that [`access-profiles`](#access-profiles) manages: an XAA profile bundles one resource server's scopes, and the API gives it a backing entitlement (`appEntitlementId`) — that entitlement is the thing users request. See [Resource servers](/product/admin/enterprise-managed-authorization/resource-servers) and [Scopes and access profiles](/product/admin/enterprise-managed-authorization/scopes-and-profiles) for the product view.

Mutations print the object unwrapped like the matching `get`, so read `.id`; deletes and `resync-scopes` print `{}`, and `--fields` is a usage error on a mutation. `history` streams one NDJSON record per change, `{"snapshot": {...}, "metadata": {...}}`, where the metadata carries `id`, `changeKind`, `createdAt`, `actor`, `annotations`, `traceId` and `syslogEventId`; the snapshot has the shape the matching `get` prints. An unknown id on `history` lists nothing and exits `0`, and a well-formed but unknown `--app-id` on `list` returns zero rows at exit `0`.

| Command | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| [`c1i xaa resource-servers list`](#xaa-resource-servers-list) | List an app's XAA resource servers. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| [`c1i xaa resource-servers search`](#xaa-resource-servers-search) | Search resource servers across the tenant. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| [`c1i xaa resource-servers get`](#xaa-resource-servers-get) | Show one resource server, with its latest scope discovery outcome. |
| [`c1i xaa resource-servers create`](#xaa-resource-servers-create) | Register a third-party authorization server as an audience of an app. |
| [`c1i xaa resource-servers update`](#xaa-resource-servers-update) | Update a resource server; the audience is immutable. |
| [`c1i xaa resource-servers delete`](#xaa-resource-servers-delete) | Soft-delete a resource server and the objects under it. |
| [`c1i xaa resource-servers history`](#xaa-resource-servers-history) | List a resource server's change history. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| [`c1i xaa resource-servers resync-scopes`](#xaa-resource-servers-resync-scopes) | Start another scope discovery run. |
| [`c1i xaa scopes list`](#xaa-scopes-list) | List an app's XAA scopes, across its resource servers. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| [`c1i xaa scopes get`](#xaa-scopes-get) | Show one scope. |
| [`c1i xaa scopes create`](#xaa-scopes-create) | Declare a scope on a resource server. |
| [`c1i xaa scopes update`](#xaa-scopes-update) | Update a scope, including approving it with `--state enabled`. |
| [`c1i xaa scopes delete`](#xaa-scopes-delete) | Soft-delete a scope. |
| [`c1i xaa scopes history`](#xaa-scopes-history) | List a scope's change history. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| [`c1i xaa access-profiles list`](#xaa-access-profiles-list) | List an app's XAA access profiles. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| [`c1i xaa access-profiles get`](#xaa-access-profiles-get) | Show one XAA access profile. |
| [`c1i xaa access-profiles create`](#xaa-access-profiles-create) | Create an XAA access profile under a resource server. |
| [`c1i xaa access-profiles update`](#xaa-access-profiles-update) | Update an XAA access profile's display name or description. |
| [`c1i xaa access-profiles delete`](#xaa-access-profiles-delete) | Soft-delete an XAA access profile. |
| [`c1i xaa access-profiles for-entitlement`](#xaa-access-profiles-for-entitlement) | Show the profile behind a backing entitlement. |
| [`c1i xaa access-profiles history`](#xaa-access-profiles-history) | List an access profile's change history. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| [`c1i xaa access-profiles scopes`](#xaa-access-profiles-scopes) | List the scopes bound to an access profile. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| [`c1i xaa access-profiles add-scopes`](#xaa-access-profiles-add-scopes) | Bind scopes to an access profile. |
| [`c1i xaa access-profiles remove-scopes`](#xaa-access-profiles-remove-scopes) | Unbind scopes from an access profile. |
| [`c1i xaa client-audience-mappings list`](#xaa-client-audience-mappings-list) | List a resource server's client audience mappings. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| [`c1i xaa client-audience-mappings search`](#xaa-client-audience-mappings-search) | Search client audience mappings across the tenant. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| [`c1i xaa client-audience-mappings get`](#xaa-client-audience-mappings-get) | Show one mapping by client key. |
| [`c1i xaa client-audience-mappings create`](#xaa-client-audience-mappings-create) | Map a client to its client ID at a resource server. |
| [`c1i xaa client-audience-mappings update`](#xaa-client-audience-mappings-update) | Update a mapping's audience client ID or `--disabled`. |
| [`c1i xaa client-audience-mappings delete`](#xaa-client-audience-mappings-delete) | Soft-delete a mapping; the key can't be mapped again. |
| [`c1i xaa client-audience-mappings history`](#xaa-client-audience-mappings-history) | List a mapping's change history. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |

`create` on all three app-scoped objects looks up `--app-id`, and for scopes and access profiles the resource server too, before sending: the server would store an object under an unknown app or an unknown or deleted resource server. An unknown one exits `4`, a deleted one `2`, and `--dry-run` does the same lookups.

### `xaa resource-servers list`

[Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | ID of the app the object belongs to. (Required.) |

Rows carry `id`, `app_id`, `display_name`, `description`, `normalized_audience`, `resource_uris`, `max_grant_lifetime`, `signing_algorithm`, `require_proof_of_possession`, `disabled`, `modify_claims_hook`, `sector_id`, `created_at` and `updated_at`. A deleted server is not listed.

### `xaa resource-servers search`

[Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id stringArray` | Only servers of this app (repeatable). |
| `--query string` | Text to match. In the run checked it matched display names, not audiences. |
| `--signing-algorithm stringArray` | Only servers with this signing algorithm (repeatable): `unspecified`, `eddsa`, `rs256`, `es256`, or any `XAA_SIGNING_ALGORITHM_*` value. `unspecified` is a server using the tenant default. |
| `--disabled` | `--disabled=true` lists only disabled servers, `--disabled=false` only enabled ones; omit it for both. |

With no filter, every server is listed, and a server created seconds ago may not be found yet. Rows match `resource-servers list`.

### `xaa resource-servers get`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i xaa resource-servers get <resource-server-id> --app-id <app-id>
```

Prints the server unwrapped — its own fields at the top level, plus `scopeDiscoveryStatus`, the outcome of the latest scope discovery run (`state`, `startedAt`, `completedAt`, `lastError`, `findings`, `importedScopeValues`). `history` snapshots omit `scopeDiscoveryStatus`, which is `get`-only. A deleted server is still returned, with `deletedAt` set; an unknown id exits `4`.

### `xaa resource-servers create`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i xaa resource-servers create --app-id <app-id> --audience <https-issuer> --display-name <name> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | ID of the app the object belongs to. (Required.) |
| `--audience string` | The server's issuer identifier; https. Immutable. (Required unless `--body-file`.) |
| `--display-name string` | Display name. (Required unless `--body-file`.) |
| `--description string` | Description. |
| `--resource-uri stringArray` | Resource identifier (RFC 8707) the server governs (repeatable; replaces the list). |
| `--max-grant-lifetime string` | Maximum grant lifetime, as a protobuf duration (`3600s`) or a Go duration (`1h`). |
| `--signing-algorithm string` | JWS algorithm for grants: `eddsa`, `rs256`, `es256`, or any `XAA_SIGNING_ALGORITHM_*` value; omit for the tenant default. |
| `--require-proof-of-possession` | Mint proof-of-possession-bound grants for clients presenting a DPoP proof. |
| `--disabled` | Reject token exchanges for this server without removing it. |
| `--body-file string` | Full create request JSON object, sent verbatim (file, or `-` for stdin). Mutually exclusive with the field flags, and must not carry `appId`/`app_id`: exit `2`. |

The audience is stored normalized (lowercase host, no trailing slash) and is immutable. It is unique across the tenant (a duplicate is a `409` and exits `2`, "is already registered by another resource server in this tenant") and it must not be the tenant's own issuer (`400`, "audience must not equal the tenant's own issuer"). Registering a server starts a scope discovery run against the audience; its outcome is `scopeDiscoveryStatus` in `get`.

`modifyClaimsHook` — a tenant function the API spec says runs before every grant is signed and can deny it — has no field flags: pass the whole request with `--body-file`. Its `functionId`, and `commitId` if given, are looked up first, and an unknown one exits `4` before anything is sent, `--dry-run` included.

Prints the created server unwrapped like `get`: the ID is `.id`.

**Example:**

```bash
c1i xaa resource-servers create --app-id <app-id> \
  --audience https://auth.example.com --display-name "Example API"
```

### `xaa resource-servers update`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i xaa resource-servers update <resource-server-id> --app-id <app-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | ID of the app the object belongs to. (Required.) |
| `--display-name string` | New display name. |
| `--description string` | New description; `""` clears it (omit to leave unchanged). |
| `--resource-uri stringArray` | Resource identifier (RFC 8707) the server governs (repeatable; replaces the list). |
| `--max-grant-lifetime string` | Maximum grant lifetime, as a protobuf or Go duration. |
| `--signing-algorithm string` | JWS algorithm for grants (same values as `create`). |
| `--require-proof-of-possession` | Mint proof-of-possession-bound grants (pass `--require-proof-of-possession=false` to stop). |
| `--disabled` | Reject token exchanges for this server without removing it (`--disabled=false` re-enables it). |
| `--body-file string` | A `resourceServer` JSON object (file, or `-` for stdin); requires `--update-mask`. Mutually exclusive with the field flags, and must not carry `appId`/`app_id`, or an `id` other than `<resource-server-id>`: exit `2`. |
| `--update-mask string` | Comma-separated field paths: required with `--body-file`, rejected without it, derived otherwise. |

Only the flags you pass change, and `--resource-uri` replaces the whole list. The audience is immutable: a mask naming it is refused by the server (`normalized_audience is immutable`). Use `--body-file` with an explicit `--update-mask` for `modifyClaimsHook`, or to clear a field (`resourceUris`, `maxGrantLifetime`, `signingAlgorithm` back to the tenant default) — a masked field the object leaves out is cleared. Name mask paths in camelCase: the server refused a wildcard or snake_case path with a `400` (exit `2`), `FieldMask.paths contains invalid path`.

The server validates `displayName` and `normalizedAudience` on every update whatever the mask names, so when the request leaves either unset c1i reads the server first and fills it in; the mask still decides what changes. That read makes an unknown id exit `4` before the update is sent, `--dry-run` included. An update of a deleted server exits `0` and applies, and `deletedAt` stays set.

### `xaa resource-servers delete`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i xaa resource-servers delete <resource-server-id> --app-id <app-id>
```

Honors `--dry-run` and prints `{}`. The delete is soft: `get` still returns the server with `deletedAt` set, and `list` and `search` stop listing it. Repeating the delete exits `0`; an unknown id exits `4`. Deleting a server deleted its scopes and access profiles, and each one's backing entitlement went with it within seconds in the run checked; the API spec says it also cascades to client audience mappings.

### `xaa resource-servers history`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i xaa resource-servers history <resource-server-id> --app-id <app-id> [--limit <n>]
```

[Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). One NDJSON record per change, newest first as the server returns them.

### `xaa resource-servers resync-scopes`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i xaa resource-servers resync-scopes <resource-server-id> --app-id <app-id>
```

Starts another scope discovery run. The API spec says the run imports newly advertised scopes in state `pending-review`, and that a request while a run is in progress queues one more run; the outcome of the latest run is `scopeDiscoveryStatus` in `get`. Honors `--dry-run` and prints `{}`. An unknown id exits `4`.

### `xaa scopes list`

[Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | ID of the app the object belongs to. (Required.) |

Rows carry `id`, `app_id`, `display_name`, `description`, `resource_server_id`, `scope_value`, `state`, `classification`, `source`, `app_entitlement_id`, `last_discovered_at`, `created_at` and `updated_at`. The list spans every resource server of the app, and a deleted scope is not listed.

### `xaa scopes get`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i xaa scopes get <scope-id> --app-id <app-id>
```

Prints the scope unwrapped. A deleted scope is still returned, with `deletedAt` set; an unknown id exits `4`.

### `xaa scopes create`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i xaa scopes create --app-id <app-id> --resource-server-id <id> \
  --scope-value <scope> --display-name <name> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | ID of the app the object belongs to. (Required.) |
| `--resource-server-id string` | ID of the resource server the scope belongs to. (Required.) |
| `--scope-value string` | Literal OAuth scope string minted into grants; immutable and unique per resource server. (Required.) |
| `--display-name string` | Display name. (Required.) |
| `--description string` | Description. |
| `--classification string` | Risk classification: `read`, `write`, `destructive`, `sensitive`, `dangerous`, or any `XAA_SCOPE_CLASSIFICATION_*` value. |
| `--state string` | Approval state: `pending-review`, `enabled`, `disabled`, or any `XAA_SCOPE_STATE_*` value. Default `pending-review`. |

A scope is mintable only in state `enabled`; a discovered or newly declared scope starts in `pending-review`, and `scopes update --state enabled` approves it. `--state removed` exits `2` before sending — the server reserves that state — so use `--state disabled` to block a scope, or delete it. A `--scope-value` already declared on the server is a `409` (exit `2`, "already exists for this resource server"). The server provisions a backing entitlement (`appEntitlementId`).

Prints the created scope unwrapped like `get`: the ID is `.id`.

**Example:**

```bash
c1i xaa scopes create --app-id <app-id> --resource-server-id <resource-server-id> \
  --scope-value orders.read --display-name "Read orders" --classification read
```

### `xaa scopes update`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i xaa scopes update <scope-id> --app-id <app-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | ID of the app the object belongs to. (Required.) |
| `--display-name string` | New display name. |
| `--description string` | New description; `""` clears it (omit to leave unchanged). |
| `--classification string` | Risk classification (same values as `create`). |
| `--state string` | Approval state. `--state enabled` makes the scope mintable, `--state disabled` blocks it; `removed` exits `2`. |

Only the flags you pass change, and the scope value and resource server are immutable. The server validates `displayName`, `scopeValue` and `xaaResourceServerId` on every update whatever the mask names, so c1i reads the scope first and fills in the ones the request leaves unset; the mask still decides what changes. That read makes an unknown id exit `4` before the update is sent, `--dry-run` included. An update of a deleted scope exits `0` and applies, and `deletedAt` stays set.

### `xaa scopes delete`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i xaa scopes delete <scope-id> --app-id <app-id>
```

Honors `--dry-run` and prints `{}`. The delete is soft: `get` still returns the scope with `deletedAt` set, and `list` stops listing it. Repeating the delete exits `0`; an unknown id exits `4`. Its backing entitlement was deleted within seconds in the run checked, and the API spec says it also cascades to access-profile bindings that reference it.

### `xaa scopes history`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i xaa scopes history <scope-id> --app-id <app-id> [--limit <n>]
```

[Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). One NDJSON record per change, newest first. There is no `xaa scopes search`: use `list --app-id`.

### `xaa access-profiles list`

[Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | ID of the app the object belongs to. (Required.) |

Rows carry `id`, `app_id`, `display_name`, `description`, `resource_server_id`, `app_entitlement_id`, `scope_count`, `created_at` and `updated_at`. A deleted profile is not listed.

### `xaa access-profiles get`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i xaa access-profiles get <access-profile-id> --app-id <app-id>
```

Prints the profile unwrapped. A deleted profile is still returned, with `deletedAt` set; an unknown id exits `4`. The scopes bound to it are listed by [`access-profiles scopes`](#xaa-access-profiles-scopes).

### `xaa access-profiles create`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i xaa access-profiles create --app-id <app-id> --resource-server-id <id> \
  --display-name <name> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | ID of the app the object belongs to. (Required.) |
| `--resource-server-id string` | ID of the resource server whose scopes the profile bundles. (Required.) |
| `--display-name string` | Display name. (Required.) |
| `--description string` | Description. |

The server provisions a backing entitlement (`appEntitlementId`), which is what users request; display names need not be unique.

**Example:**

```bash
c1i xaa access-profiles create --app-id <app-id> \
  --resource-server-id <resource-server-id> --display-name "Orders read-only"
```

### `xaa access-profiles update`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i xaa access-profiles update <access-profile-id> --app-id <app-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | ID of the app the object belongs to. (Required.) |
| `--display-name string` | New display name. |
| `--description string` | New description; `""` clears it (omit to leave unchanged). |

Only the flags you pass change. The server validates `displayName` and `xaaResourceServerId` on every update whatever the mask names, so c1i reads the profile first and fills in the ones the request leaves unset; that read makes an unknown id exit `4` before the update is sent, `--dry-run` included.

### `xaa access-profiles delete`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i xaa access-profiles delete <access-profile-id> --app-id <app-id>
```

Honors `--dry-run` and prints `{}`. The delete is soft: `get` still returns the profile with `deletedAt` set, and `list` stops listing it. Repeating the delete exits `0`; an unknown id exits `4`. Its backing entitlement was deleted within seconds in the run checked, and the API spec says outstanding grants end with the profile.

### `xaa access-profiles for-entitlement`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i xaa access-profiles for-entitlement <entitlement-id> --app-id <app-id>
```

Shows the profile whose backing entitlement is `<entitlement-id>`, unwrapped like `get`. An entitlement with no profile exits `4`; in the run checked, so did a profile created seconds earlier.

### `xaa access-profiles history`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i xaa access-profiles history <access-profile-id> --app-id <app-id> [--limit <n>]
```

[Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). One NDJSON record per change, newest first.

### `xaa access-profiles scopes`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i xaa access-profiles scopes <access-profile-id> --app-id <app-id> [--limit <n>]
```

[Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). One row per binding: `access_profile_id`, `scope_id`, `resource_server_id`, `app_id`, `created_at`, `updated_at`. Change them with `add-scopes` and `remove-scopes`. A well-formed but unknown profile returns zero rows at exit `0`.

### `xaa access-profiles add-scopes`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i xaa access-profiles add-scopes <access-profile-id> --app-id <app-id> \
  --scope-id <scope-id> [--scope-id <scope-id> ...]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | ID of the app the object belongs to. (Required.) |
| `--scope-id stringArray` | ID of a scope of the profile's resource server (repeatable). (Required.) |

Every scope must belong to the profile's resource server; the server refuses one that does not ("belongs to a different resource server than access profile", exit `2`). A repeated `--scope-id` is sent once, the server takes at most 100 ids per request, and binding an already bound scope exits `0`. Each scope is looked up first under `--app-id`: an unknown one exits `4` and a deleted one `2`, nothing sent, `--dry-run` included. Prints `{"bindings": [...]}`, one object per bound scope.

### `xaa access-profiles remove-scopes`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i xaa access-profiles remove-scopes <access-profile-id> --app-id <app-id> \
  --scope-id <scope-id> [--scope-id <scope-id> ...]
```

Takes the same flags as `add-scopes` and prints `{}`. Removing a scope that is not bound exits `0`, and a repeated `--scope-id` is sent once. Each scope is looked up first under `--app-id`: an unknown one exits `4`, nothing sent (the server answers it with a silent success), while a deleted scope is accepted.

### `xaa client-audience-mappings list`

[Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--resource-server-id string` | ID of the resource server the mapping belongs to. (Required.) |

One row per mapping: `resource_server_id`, `client_key`, `audience_client_id`, `disabled`, `created_at`, `updated_at`. A deleted mapping is not listed, and a well-formed but unknown resource server returns zero rows at exit `0`.

### `xaa client-audience-mappings search`

[Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--resource-server-id stringArray` | Only mappings of this resource server (repeatable). An unknown one matches nothing. |
| `--query string` | Text to match. In the run checked it matched part of the client key and of the audience client ID. |
| `--disabled` | `--disabled=true` lists only disabled mappings, `--disabled=false` only enabled ones; omit it for both. |

With no filter, every mapping is listed, and a deleted one is not. A mapping created seconds ago may not be found yet. Rows match `list`.

### `xaa client-audience-mappings get`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i xaa client-audience-mappings get <client-key> --resource-server-id <id>
```

Prints the mapping unwrapped. A mapping's ID is its client key, which may be a URL, so c1i sends it as a query parameter or in the body, never in the path. A deleted mapping is still returned, with `deletedAt` set; an unknown key exits `4`.

### `xaa client-audience-mappings create`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i xaa client-audience-mappings create --resource-server-id <id> \
  --client-key <client-key> --audience-client-id <client-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--resource-server-id string` | ID of the resource server the mapping belongs to. (Required.) |
| `--client-key string` | Client key: a CIMD client ID URL, `dcr://<client_id>` or `c1://<service_principal_id>`. Immutable. (Required.) |
| `--audience-client-id string` | The client's client ID at the resource server. (Required.) |
| `--disabled` | Create the mapping with the client's exchanges rejected. |

Each mapping maps a client, named by its client key, to its client ID at that server, which the API spec says is stamped into the `client_id` claim of grants C1 mints for it; `--disabled` rejects the client's exchanges for the server without removing the mapping. The server refused other key forms with "client_key is not a recognized form (expected a CIMD url, dcr://, c1://, or a /dcc, /mdc, or /sso cutename)".

A key already mapped under the server exits `2` ("a client mapping for this client key already exists; edit it instead"), and so does a key whose mapping was deleted ("was deleted and can't be recreated"). The resource server is looked up first with a tenant-wide resource-server search, because the server stores a mapping under an unknown resource server and the mapping routes carry no app: a server the search does not return exits `4`, nothing sent, `--dry-run` included. Search omits deleted servers, and a server created a moment earlier was missing from it for about a second in the run checked; retry then.

**Example:**

```bash
c1i xaa client-audience-mappings create --resource-server-id <resource-server-id> \
  --client-key https://client.example.com/metadata.json --audience-client-id orders-agent
```

### `xaa client-audience-mappings update`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i xaa client-audience-mappings update <client-key> --resource-server-id <id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--resource-server-id string` | ID of the resource server the mapping belongs to. (Required.) |
| `--audience-client-id string` | New client ID at the resource server. |
| `--disabled` | Reject the client's exchanges for this server without removing the mapping (`--disabled=false` re-enables them). |

Only the flags you pass change, and the client key and resource server are immutable. The server validates `audienceClientId` on every update whatever the mask names, so without `--audience-client-id` c1i reads the mapping first and fills it in; that read makes an unknown key exit `4` before the update is sent, `--dry-run` included. An update of a deleted mapping exits `0` and applies, and `deletedAt` stays set.

### `xaa client-audience-mappings delete`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i xaa client-audience-mappings delete <client-key> --resource-server-id <id>
```

Honors `--dry-run` and prints `{}`. The delete is soft: `get` still returns the mapping with `deletedAt` set, `list` and `search` stop listing it, and `update` still changes it. Repeating the delete exits `0`; an unknown key exits `4`. The key cannot be mapped again under the same server.

### `xaa client-audience-mappings history`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i xaa client-audience-mappings history <client-key> --resource-server-id <id> [--limit <n>]
```

[Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). One NDJSON record per change, newest first. An unknown id lists nothing and exits `0`.

## Bridges

Manage tunnel bridges: the tenant-side record a tunnel appliance authenticates against. A bridge's appliance presents a client credential created on the bridge, and reports the services it announces behind it. The group is aliased as `bridge`, and its credential subgroup as `credentials`, `credential`, or `creds`.

| Command | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| [`c1i bridges list`](#bridges-list-and-bridges-get) | List tunnel bridges, with appliance status and last-seen time. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| [`c1i bridges get`](#bridges-list-and-bridges-get) | Get a single bridge. A deleted or unknown bridge exits `4`. |
| [`c1i bridges create`](#bridges-create-and-bridges-update) | Create a bridge. |
| [`c1i bridges update`](#bridges-create-and-bridges-update) | Change a bridge's display name or description. |
| [`c1i bridges delete`](#bridges-delete) | Delete a bridge. |
| [`c1i bridges services`](#bridges-services) | List the services a bridge's appliance announces. |
| [`c1i bridges credentials list`](#bridges-credentials) | List a bridge's client credentials. A revoked credential is listed with its status. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| [`c1i bridges credentials create`](#bridges-credentials) | Create a client credential. The secret is returned once, here. |
| [`c1i bridges credentials revoke`](#bridges-credentials) | Revoke a client credential. |

Two of the reads answer an unknown `<bridge-id>` with an empty list at exit `0`: `bridges services` and `bridges credentials list` print nothing. `bridges delete` likewise prints `{}` at exit `0` for a bridge that is already deleted or does not exist.

### `bridges list` and `bridges get`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i bridges list [flags]
c1i bridges get <bridge-id>
```

[Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). A row carries `id`, `display_name`, `description`, `appliance_status`, `last_seen_at`, `announced_service_count`, `created_at`, and `updated_at`. `get` prints the bridge as JSON.

### `bridges create` and `bridges update`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i bridges create --display-name <name> [flags]
c1i bridges update <bridge-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--display-name string` | Display name. It can't be blank: empty, whitespace-only, or invisible-only. (Required for `create`.) |
| `--description string` | Description. On `update`, an empty value is sent as an empty description. |

`update` sends only the flags you pass; an unknown bridge exits `4`. Both commands print the bridge unwrapped like `bridges get`, so its id is `.id`.

```bash
c1i bridges create --display-name "datacenter-1" --description "Primary tunnel appliance"
```

### `bridges services`

List the services the bridge's appliance announces, as NDJSON.

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--limit int` | Print only the first N services; `0` prints all (default `0`). |

Rows are `name`, `port`, `service_type`, `service_path`, and `transport_type`.

### `bridges credentials`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i bridges credentials list <bridge-id> [flags]
c1i bridges credentials create <bridge-id>
c1i bridges credentials revoke <credential-id>
```

`create` prints the credential as JSON with `.id`, `.clientId`, and `.clientSecret` side by side. Capture `.clientSecret` immediately: `credentials list` never returns it. A deleted or unknown bridge exits `4`.

`credentials list` [paginates](#pagination-and-limits) and lists a revoked credential with `credential_status` `TUNNEL_CREDENTIAL_STATUS_REVOKED`. Rows are `id`, `bridge_id`, `client_id`, `credential_status`, `created_at`, `expires_time`, `last_used_at`, and `revoked_at`.

`revoke` prints `{}`. Revoking an already revoked credential also prints `{}` at exit `0`; an unknown credential exits `4`.

### `bridges delete`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i bridges delete <bridge-id>
```

Prints `{}`; exit `0` is the success signal.

## Webhooks

Manage outbound webhooks: the registered https endpoints that C1 calls, for example from a policy's webhook approval or provision step. The group is aliased as `webhook`. A webhook has no signing secret, so `create` and `get` return the same fields.

| Command | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `c1i webhooks list` | List webhooks. Deleted webhooks are omitted. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| `c1i webhooks get <webhook-id>` | Get a single webhook by ID. A deleted webhook is still returned, with `deletedAt` set. |
| [`c1i webhooks search`](#webhooks-search) | Search webhooks by text or id. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| [`c1i webhooks create`](#webhooks-create-and-webhooks-update) | Create a webhook. |
| [`c1i webhooks update`](#webhooks-create-and-webhooks-update) | Update a webhook's display name, URL, description, or callback timeout. |
| [`c1i webhooks test`](#webhooks-test) | Queue a test delivery to a webhook's URL. |
| [`c1i webhooks delete`](#webhooks-delete) | Soft-delete a webhook. |

`webhooks list` returns pages larger than `--page-size`: the server raises a size below 5 to 5 and returns one row more than that, so use `--limit` for an exact count.

### `webhooks create` and `webhooks update`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i webhooks create --display-name <name> --destination-url <url> [flags]
c1i webhooks update <webhook-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--display-name string` | Display name. Can't be blank, and the server requires 3-100 characters. (Required for `create`.) |
| `--destination-url string` | Destination URL. It must be an absolute `https` URL with a host; an IPv6 literal host is refused. (Required for `create`.) |
| `--description string` | Description. On `update`, an empty value clears it. |
| `--callback-timeout string` | Time to wait for an asynchronous callback, as a whole-number Go duration such as `10m` or a protobuf duration such as `600s`; c1i sends it in seconds. Accepts `0` to `720h`. |

The endpoint flag is `--destination-url`, not `--url`, which stays the global tenant flag. These cases exit with code 2 before c1i sends anything: a `--destination-url` that isn't `https`, has no host, or uses an IPv6 literal host; a blank `--display-name`; a `--callback-timeout` with a sign or decimal (`-1h`, `1.5h`); and an `update` with no field flag.

`update` sends only the flags you pass. Updating a soft-deleted webhook exits `0` and changes it, and it stays deleted.

Both commands print the webhook as formatted JSON, unwrapped like `webhooks get`, so its ID is `.id`.

**Example:**

```bash
c1i webhooks create --display-name "ticketing-bridge" \
  --destination-url https://hooks.example.com/c1
```

### `webhooks search`

Search webhooks by text or id.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i webhooks search [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--query string` | Text to match against webhook names. The API describes the match as covering names and descriptions, but a word that occurred only in a description matched nothing when tried. |
| `--webhook-id stringArray` | Restrict the search to this webhook id (repeatable). |

[Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). Rows are the same fields `webhooks list` emits. With neither flag it returns every webhook; with both, a webhook must satisfy both.

The search index lags the writes: a new webhook was missing from an unfiltered search for a few seconds, and a deleted one may still be found briefly. Use `webhooks list` or `webhooks get` for the current state.

### `webhooks test`

Queue a test delivery to a webhook's URL.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i webhooks test <webhook-id>
```

The delivery is asynchronous. The command prints the queued delivery with `"state": "WEBHOOK_STATE_PENDING"` and exits `0` whether or not the endpoint is reachable, so exit `0` doesn't mean the endpoint answered. In the output, `.id` is the delivery and `.webhookId` is the webhook you tested.

### `webhooks delete`

Soft-delete a webhook. It leaves `webhooks list`, but `webhooks get` still returns it at exit `0` with `deletedAt` set. Deleting it again exits `0` again. Prints `{}`; exit `0` means success.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i webhooks delete <webhook-id>
```

## Hooks

Manage hooks: customer-configured interception points for tool calls. A hook fires on an event (`pre-tool-use`, `post-tool-use`, or `pre-output`), optionally narrowed by a CEL filter, and runs exactly one of a function, a built-in pattern, or a JSON patch. The group is aliased as `hook`.

The flags build a function hook. A built-in pattern or JSON patch hook, whose configs the flags don't model, goes through `--body-file`: a full Hook object. A hook is created disabled unless you pass `--enabled`, and only an enabled hook intercepts tool calls.

| Command | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| [`c1i hooks list`](#hooks-list-and-hooks-get) | List hooks. Deleted hooks are omitted. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| [`c1i hooks get`](#hooks-list-and-hooks-get) | Get a single hook with its full config: filter, function, built-in pattern. |
| [`c1i hooks create`](#hooks-create) | Create a hook. |
| [`c1i hooks update`](#hooks-update) | Update a hook's fields. |
| [`c1i hooks delete`](#hooks-delete) | Soft-delete a hook. |

There is no `hooks search`: the API answers a query with `UNIMPLEMENTED`, so `hooks list` is the way to find a hook.

### `hooks list` and `hooks get`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i hooks list [flags]
c1i hooks get <hook-id>
```

[Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). A list row carries `id`, `display_name`, `description`, `event`, `kind` (`function`, `builtin_pattern`, or `json_patch`), `enabled`, `managed_by_guardrails`, `priority`, `created_at`, and `updated_at`. `get` prints the whole hook, including the config a row only summarizes.

### `hooks create`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i hooks create --display-name <name> --event <event> --function-id <function-id> [flags]
c1i hooks create --body-file <file|->
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--display-name string` | Hook display name. It can't be blank: empty, whitespace-only, or invisible-only. (Required unless `--body-file`.) |
| `--event string` | Hook event: `pre-tool-use`, `post-tool-use`, `pre-output`, or any `HOOK_EVENT_TYPE_*` value. (Required unless `--body-file`.) |
| `--function-id string` | Function to run. (Required unless `--body-file`.) |
| `--function-commit-id string` | Function commit to run. Requires `--function-id`; omit it for the function's published commit. |
| `--description string` | Description; an empty value is sent as an empty description. |
| `--filter-cel string` | CEL filter, evaluated against the event context. The server does not validate it. |
| `--priority int` | Priority, `0` to `1000`. |
| `--enabled` | Enable the hook. Without it the hook is created disabled. |
| `--managed-by-guardrails` | Make the hook selectable in a guardrail rule's curated hook lists. A hook left `false` always runs. |
| `--body-file string` | Full Hook JSON object, sent verbatim (file, or `-` for stdin). Mutually exclusive with the field flags. |

`--function-id` and `--function-commit-id` are looked up before the request, `--dry-run` included: an unknown one exits `4`, and a soft-deleted function or commit exits `2`. The check matters because the server answers an unknown function with a `400`, yet stores an unknown commit id at exit `0`. A `--body-file` is sent verbatim and not checked.

The server refuses a hook that sets none of the type fields (`hook function reference is missing function_id`), and refuses a JSON patch hook with `403` when its preview is not enabled for the tenant. `--priority` outside `0`-`1000` exits `2` before sending.

`create` prints the hook unwrapped like `hooks get`, so its id is `.id`.

```bash
c1i hooks create --display-name "audit-tool-calls" --event pre-tool-use \
  --function-id <function-id> --priority 10 --enabled
```

### `hooks update`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i hooks update <hook-id> [flags]
c1i hooks update <hook-id> --body-file <file|-> --update-mask <paths>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--display-name string` | New display name. It can't be blank. |
| `--description string` | New description; an empty value is sent as an empty description. |
| `--event string` | New event: `pre-tool-use`, `post-tool-use`, `pre-output`, or any `HOOK_EVENT_TYPE_*` value. |
| `--filter-cel string` | New CEL filter. The server does not validate it. |
| `--clear-filter` | Remove the CEL filter. Mutually exclusive with `--filter-cel`. |
| `--priority int` | New priority, `0` to `1000`. |
| `--enabled` | Enable (`--enabled`) or disable (`--enabled=false`) the hook. |
| `--managed-by-guardrails` | Make the hook selectable in a guardrail rule's curated hook lists. |
| `--function-id string` | New function to run. Replaces the whole function reference. |
| `--function-commit-id string` | New function commit. Requires `--function-id`. |
| `--body-file string` | Full Hook JSON object, sent verbatim (file, or `-` for stdin). Requires `--update-mask`. |
| `--update-mask string` | Comma-separated field paths. Required with `--body-file` and rejected without it; otherwise derived from the flags you pass. |

`update` sends only the flags you pass, and each is named in the mask. `--function-id` resets the commit to the function's published one unless `--function-commit-id` is given; both are looked up first, as on `create`.

Exit `2` before any request: no field flag at all, a blank `--display-name`, `--clear-filter` together with `--filter-cel`, `--function-commit-id` without `--function-id`, and a `--body-file` priority outside `0`-`1000` when the mask names `priority` (update does not validate the value server-side, so c1i checks it). A `--body-file` that carries an `id` other than `<hook-id>` also exits `2`.

The server leaves a field alone when its path is not in the mask, and accepts an unknown mask path at exit `0` without changing anything. Editing a deleted hook succeeds and changes it; the hook stays out of `hooks list`. `update` prints the hook unwrapped like `hooks get`.

```bash
# Clear the CEL filter and reprioritize
c1i hooks update <hook-id> --clear-filter --priority 5

# Edit a built-in pattern hook's config
c1i hooks update <hook-id> --body-file hook.json --update-mask builtinPattern
```

### `hooks delete`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i hooks delete <hook-id>
```

Soft-deletes the hook: it leaves `hooks list`, but `hooks get` still returns it at exit `0`. The Hook has no `deletedAt`, so nothing in the returned object marks it deleted. Deleting it again exits `0`. Prints `{}`; exit `0` is the success signal. The API says a hook that a guardrail rule references cannot be deleted until that reference is removed.

## Settings

Inspect and update tenant-wide settings: AI governance, session security, the SSO provider, cross-app access, contacts, access requests, verified domains, notifications, and the AWS external id.

Each area is a singleton, so no area has an id. `show` prints it as pretty JSON, unwrapped from its envelope, and `update` prints the updated object the same way. Masked updates take field flags, and c1i derives the update mask from the flags you pass. Anything the flags don't cover goes through `--body-file` — the object `show` prints — with an explicit `--update-mask` of camelCase paths. A field the mask doesn't name is left alone.

A boolean flag is tri-state: omit it to leave the field unchanged, pass `--flag` to set it `true`, or `--flag=false` to set it `false`.

Two operations replace a whole list rather than editing it: the repeatable list flags (`--allowed-client-type`, `--enabled-signing-algorithm`, the contact email flags) send exactly the values you give, and `settings domains set` replaces the entire domain set.

| Command | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| [`c1i settings ai-governance show`](#settings-ai-governance) | Show AI governance settings. |
| [`c1i settings ai-governance update`](#settings-ai-governance) | Update AI governance settings. |
| [`c1i settings ai-governance history`](#settings-ai-governance) | List the AI governance settings change history, newest first. |
| [`c1i settings ai-governance tenant-defaults`](#settings-ai-governance) | Show the legacy tenant-default subset of AI governance settings. |
| [`c1i settings session show`](#settings-session) | Show session security settings. |
| [`c1i settings session update`](#settings-session) | Update session security settings. |
| [`c1i settings session test-source-ip`](#settings-session) | Check whether an IP would pass a CIDR allowlist. |
| [`c1i settings sso show`](#settings-sso) | Show the SSO provider settings. |
| [`c1i settings sso update`](#settings-sso) | Update the SSO provider settings. |
| [`c1i settings sso history`](#settings-sso) | List the SSO settings change history, newest first. |
| [`c1i settings cross-app-access show`](#settings-cross-app-access) | Show cross-app access settings. |
| [`c1i settings cross-app-access update`](#settings-cross-app-access) | Update cross-app access settings. |
| [`c1i settings cross-app-access history`](#settings-cross-app-access) | List the cross-app access settings change history, newest first. |
| [`c1i settings contacts show`](#settings-contacts) | Show the security, billing, and operations contact emails. |
| [`c1i settings contacts update`](#settings-contacts) | Replace one or more contact email lists. |
| [`c1i settings request show`](#settings-request) | Show access-request settings. |
| [`c1i settings request update`](#settings-request) | Update access-request settings. |
| [`c1i settings domains list`](#settings-domains) | List the tenant's verified domains. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| [`c1i settings domains set`](#settings-domains) | Replace the tenant's verified domains. |
| [`c1i settings notifications org show`](#settings-notifications) | Show organization-level notification settings. |
| [`c1i settings notifications org update`](#settings-notifications) | Update organization-level notification settings. |
| [`c1i settings notifications user show`](#settings-notifications) | Show your own notification preferences. |
| [`c1i settings notifications user update`](#settings-notifications) | Update your own notification preferences. |
| [`c1i settings aws-external-id show`](#settings-aws-external-id) | Show the tenant's AWS external id. |

### `settings ai-governance`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i settings ai-governance show
c1i settings ai-governance update [flags]
c1i settings ai-governance history [--limit N]
c1i settings ai-governance tenant-defaults
```

`show` prints the settings unwrapped from the `aiGovernanceSettings` envelope.

`update` takes these field flags. Only the flags you pass change; the mask is derived from them.

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--allowed-client-type stringArray` | MCP client type allowed to connect (repeatable; replaces the list): `personal`, `shared`, `service`, `ephemeral`, or any `MCP_CLIENT_TYPE_*` value. |
| `--default-tool-classification string` | Classification for newly discovered tools: `read`, `write`, `destructive`, `sensitive`, `dangerous`, or any `TOOL_CLASSIFICATION_*` value. |
| `--audit-verbosity string` | Audit detail for MCP tool calls: `minimal`, `standard`, `full`, or any `AUDIT_VERBOSITY_*` value. |
| `--auto-discovery` | Periodically re-discover tools from registered MCP servers (`--auto-discovery=false` to stop). |
| `--discovery-interval string` | Auto-discovery interval, as `3600s` or `8h`. |
| `--inactivity-hide-after string` | Hide an MCP client after this long inactive, as `3600s` or `8h`. |
| `--inactivity-close-after string` | Close an MCP client's access after this long inactive, as `3600s` or `8h`. |
| `--retention-delete-after string` | Remove an MCP client's record after this long inactive, as `3600s` or `8h`. |
| `--prefer-code-mode` | Set `preferCodeModeOverDirectTools`. |
| `--surface-requestable-tools` | Set `surfaceRequestableTools`. |
| `--untrusted-judge-disable` | Set `untrustedJudgeDisable`. |
| `--body-file string` | Settings JSON object as `show` prints it, sent verbatim (file, or `-` for stdin). Requires `--update-mask`. Mutually exclusive with the field flags. |
| `--update-mask string` | Comma-separated field paths. Required with `--body-file` and rejected without it; otherwise derived from the flags you pass. |

The three lifecycle flags each set one `defaultClientLifecycle` duration and leave the other two as they are. `--allowed-client-type` replaces the whole list; per the API schema an empty list allows every type, and since a flag cannot send one, use `--body-file` with `--update-mask allowedClientTypes` to do that.

Any other field, such as `codeModeConcurrency` or `usageAttribution*`, goes through `--body-file` with `--update-mask`. An unknown camelCase mask path is a `400`, `invalid update_mask`, and a snake_case path is a `400`, `FieldMask.paths contains invalid path`; both exit `2` without changing anything.

`history` writes one NDJSON record per change, newest first, as the server returns them. Each record carries the change metadata (`id`, `actor`, `changeKind`, `createdAt`, `traceId`, and so on) and the full settings snapshot. The endpoint takes no page size or page token and returns the whole history, so only `--limit` applies.

`tenant-defaults` prints the legacy tenant-default subset of the settings (`requireToolApproval`) as the server returns it. Because the object is not projected, an explicit `--fields` is a usage error (exit `2`).

### `settings session`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i settings session show
c1i settings session update [flags]
c1i settings session test-source-ip --ip <address> [flags]
```

`show` prints the maximum session length, idle timeout, external-client settings, client ID metadata document policy, and the per-surface source-IP allowlists (`ssoAdminSource`, `ssoUserSource`, `pccAdminSource`, `pccUserSource`, `connectorSource`, `externalClientSource`).

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--max-session-length string` | Maximum session length, as `3600s` or `8h`. The server accepts only some lengths and lists them when it refuses one (exit `2`). |
| `--enable-idle-timeout` | End idle sessions after `--idle-timeout` (`--enable-idle-timeout=false` to stop). |
| `--idle-timeout string` | Idle timeout, as `3600s` or `8h`. |
| `--external-clients-enabled` | Allow external clients (`--external-clients-enabled=false` to refuse them). |
| `--client-id-metadata-document-policy string` | `allow-all`, `requestable`, `allowlist-only`, or any `CLIENT_ID_METADATA_DOCUMENT_POLICY_*` value. |
| `--body-file string` | Settings JSON object as `show` prints it, sent verbatim (file, or `-` for stdin). Requires `--update-mask`. Mutually exclusive with the field flags. |
| `--update-mask string` | Comma-separated field paths. Required with `--body-file` and rejected without it; otherwise derived from the flags you pass. |

The source-IP allowlists and `clientIdApprovalRequestPolicyId` have no flags: send them with `--body-file` and `--update-mask`, for example with `ssoAdminSource` in the mask.

An allowlist that leaves out your own address can lock you out, so check it with `settings session test-source-ip` before you save it.

`test-source-ip` asks the server whether an address would pass the CIDR rules you give it. It is a read: it changes nothing, and it does not consult the saved allowlists.

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--ip string` | IP address to check. (Required.) |
| `--allow-cidr stringArray` | CIDR rule to check against (repeatable). |

```bash
c1i settings session test-source-ip --ip 203.0.113.7 --allow-cidr 203.0.113.0/24
```

It prints `allowed` (`true` or `false`), `checkedIp`, and `details` (the reason when refused, such as `unauthorized source ip`). It exits `0` for either answer, so read `.allowed`. With no `--allow-cidr` the server answers `allowed: false`; a malformed IP is a `400` (exit `2`).

### `settings sso`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i settings sso show
c1i settings sso update [flags]
c1i settings sso history [flags]
```

`show` prints `enabled`, `defaultSubjectType`, `defaultAssertionLifetime`, and `defaultIdTokenSignedResponseAlg`.

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--enabled` | Enable the SSO provider (`--enabled=false` to disable). |
| `--default-subject-type string` | `pairwise`, `public`, `compatibility`, or any `SSO_SUBJECT_TYPE_*` value. |
| `--default-assertion-lifetime string` | Default assertion lifetime, as `3600s` or `8h`. |
| `--default-id-token-alg string` | `eddsa`, `es256`, `rs256`, or any `OIDC_SIGNING_ALGORITHM_*` value. |
| `--body-file string` | Settings JSON object as `show` prints it, sent verbatim (file, or `-` for stdin). Requires `--update-mask`. Mutually exclusive with the field flags. |
| `--update-mask string` | Comma-separated field paths. Required with `--body-file` and rejected without it; otherwise derived from the flags you pass. |

On a tenant without the SSO provider feature the server refuses every update with `403`, `feature not enabled` (exit `3`).

`history` [paginates](#pagination-and-limits) and writes one NDJSON record per change, newest first: the change metadata plus the settings snapshot.

### `settings cross-app-access`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i settings cross-app-access show
c1i settings cross-app-access update [flags]
c1i settings cross-app-access history [flags]
```

`show` prints `enabled`, the grant and ID token lifetimes, the refresh-token subject switch, and the signing algorithms.

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--enabled` | Enable cross-app access (`--enabled=false` to disable). |
| `--default-grant-lifetime string` | Default grant lifetime, as `3600s` or `8h`. |
| `--xaa-id-token-lifetime string` | XAA ID token lifetime, as `3600s` or `8h`. |
| `--default-signing-algorithm string` | `eddsa`, `rs256`, `es256`, or any `XAA_SIGNING_ALGORITHM_*` value. |
| `--enabled-signing-algorithm stringArray` | Enabled signing algorithm (repeatable; replaces the list): `eddsa`, `rs256`, `es256`, or any `XAA_SIGNING_ALGORITHM_*` value. |
| `--allow-refresh-token-subjects` | Set `allowRefreshTokenSubjects`. |
| `--body-file string` | Settings JSON object as `show` prints it, sent verbatim (file, or `-` for stdin). Requires `--update-mask`. Mutually exclusive with the field flags. |
| `--update-mask string` | Comma-separated field paths. Required with `--body-file` and rejected without it; otherwise derived from the flags you pass. |

`history` [paginates](#pagination-and-limits) and writes one NDJSON record per change, newest first: the change metadata plus the settings snapshot.

### `settings contacts`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i settings contacts show
c1i settings contacts update [flags]
```

`show` prints `securityEmails`, `billingEmails`, and `operationsEmails`.

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--security-email stringArray` | Security contact email (repeatable; replaces the list). |
| `--billing-email stringArray` | Billing contact email (repeatable; replaces the list). |
| `--operations-email stringArray` | Operations contact email (repeatable; replaces the list). |
| `--body-file string` | Settings JSON object as `show` prints it, sent verbatim (file, or `-` for stdin). Requires `--update-mask`. Mutually exclusive with the field flags. |
| `--update-mask string` | Comma-separated field paths. Required with `--body-file` and rejected without it; otherwise derived from the flags you pass. |

Each list flag you pass replaces that whole list; lists you don't name are left as they are. The server checks each address and refuses an invalid one (exit `2`). To empty a list, use `--body-file` with an object that omits it: `{}` with `--update-mask securityEmails`.

### `settings request`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i settings request show
c1i settings request update [flags]
```

`show` prints `skipJustification` and `maxBulkEntitlementSelection`.

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--skip-justification` | Let requesters skip the justification (`--skip-justification=false` to require it). |
| `--max-bulk-entitlement-selection int` | Most entitlements one bulk request may select. The server bounds it to `[0, 50]` (exit `2`); `0` reads back as the server default `10`. |
| `--body-file string` | Settings JSON object as `show` prints it, sent verbatim (file, or `-` for stdin). Requires `--update-mask`. Mutually exclusive with the field flags. |
| `--update-mask string` | Comma-separated field paths. Required with `--body-file` and rejected without it; otherwise derived from the flags you pass. |

### `settings domains`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i settings domains list [flags]
c1i settings domains set --domain <domain> [flags]
c1i settings domains set --body-file <file|->
```

`list` [paginates](#pagination-and-limits); rows are `id`, `domain`, `created_at`, `updated_at`, and `deleted_at`.

`set` replaces the tenant's whole set of verified domains with exactly the `--domain` values, so any domain you leave out is removed. List the current set first and pass every domain you want to keep; a domain already in the set keeps its id.

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--domain stringArray` | Domain to keep or add (repeatable; the set is replaced by exactly these). |
| `--body-file string` | JSON request object, for example `{"newDomains": [...]}` (file, or `-` for stdin). Mutually exclusive with `--domain`. |

`--body-file` is the whole request. To empty the set, send `{"newDomains": []}`. A body without a `newDomains` array (missing or `null`) exits `2` and nothing is sent, as does an element that is not a string or is blank. The server does not check that a value looks like a domain.

`set` prints the response as pretty JSON: `{"list": [...]}` with the resulting set.

### `settings notifications`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i settings notifications org show
c1i settings notifications org update --body-file <file|->
c1i settings notifications user show
c1i settings notifications user update --body-file <file|->
```

`org` holds the organization-level preferences, which apply to every user of the tenant, and the admin-locked defaults. `user` holds the calling user's own preferences, merged with the organization-level defaults. Both `show` commands print per-channel (`email`, `slack`, `teams`) preferences.

Both `update` commands take `--body-file` only, and it is the whole request: `{"channelSettings": {...}}`, the object `show` prints. There is no update mask, so a body without a non-empty `channelSettings` object exits `2` and nothing is sent.

A partial body changes only the settings it names. Sending settings back can materialize an event the read showed as `null`, such as `system`, as its default value.

### `settings aws-external-id`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i settings aws-external-id show
```

Prints `{"externalId": "..."}`: the external ID to put in an IAM role trust policy for an AWS connector.

## Reports

Manage saved reports of the C1 reporting service: a question, the program that answers it, and the parameters a re-run may vary. There is no `create`: the API saves a report only from an already-rendered reporting surface, which needs a session in the C1 UI. Per the API, reports are not scoped by author: any caller holding the permission may delete any report in the tenant.

These commands are not the access-review reports of [`access-reviews reports generate`](#access-reviews-reports-generate).

| Command | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| [`c1i reports list`](#reports-list-and-reports-get) | List saved reports, newest first. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| [`c1i reports get`](#reports-list-and-reports-get) | Get a report with its latest run and latest successful run. |
| [`c1i reports run`](#reports-run) | Re-run the report's pinned program. |
| [`c1i reports update`](#reports-update) | Change a report's display name, description, or prompt. |
| [`c1i reports program`](#reports-program-and-reports-provenance) | Show the report's saved program source. |
| [`c1i reports provenance`](#reports-program-and-reports-provenance) | Explain one run: what it read and the program it ran. |
| [`c1i reports delete`](#reports-delete) | Delete a report and its saved program. |

A run's `artifactUrl` may be a signed URL. Treat it as a secret: it is not a durable link.

### `reports list` and `reports get`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i reports list [flags]
c1i reports get <report-id>
```

[Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). A list row is the report as the API returns it, including `latestRunSummary`. `get` prints the report unwrapped, so `latestRun`, `latestSuccessfulRun`, and `promptDrifted` sit beside `.id` at the top level.

### `reports run`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i reports run <report-id>
```

Re-runs the report's pinned program against current data. Per the API it never re-plans, and it returns as soon as the invocation starts: the printed run has a `phase` and `status` that are not final. Poll with `reports get <report-id>`. There is no `--wait`.

Prints the run unwrapped, so its id is `.id`. If writing that response fails, c1i says the run was not delivered.

### `reports update`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i reports update <report-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--display-name string` | New display name. It can't be blank: empty, whitespace-only, or invisible-only. |
| `--description string` | New description. |
| `--prompt string` | New prompt. |

At least one flag is required, and a blank value exits `2`. The API refuses a request whose fields are all empty. Editing `--prompt` does not re-plan the report, so the prompt may drift from the one its program was planned with. Parameter values are not editable here.

Prints the report unwrapped, so its id is `.id`.

### `reports program` and `reports provenance`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i reports program <report-id> --program-id <program-id>
c1i reports provenance <run-id> --report-id <report-id>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--program-id string` | Program id to show. (Required for `program`.) |
| `--report-id string` | Report the run belongs to. (Required for `provenance`.) |

`program` prints the report's durable source: a `programId` and a map of file names to contents, which can be large. `--program-id` is required; the API answers its absence with a `400`.

`provenance` explains a run: its sources, pinned program id, input, and program source, as the API returns them.

### `reports delete`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i reports delete <report-id>
```

Deletes the report. Per the API, its saved program is removed with it, so it can no longer be run. Prints `{}`; exit `0` is the success signal.

## MCP gateway

Call the C1 MCP gateway directly over its MCP transport — the same handshake an MCP host performs — to verify what a registered server actually exposes. This closes the configure-then-verify loop: register a server, approve its tools, then list or call them here.

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--gateway-url string` | MCP gateway endpoint (default: derived from `--url` by inserting `-mcp` into the host — `https://acme.conductor.one` becomes `https://acme-mcp.conductor.one/v1`). |

The gateway accepts your stored c1i credentials, so no extra setup is needed.

### `mcp gateway list-tools`

Run the MCP handshake against the gateway and list the tools it exposes to you. Outputs NDJSON: one row per tool (name, description). A gateway cursor that never ends exits with code 8.

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--full` | Include each tool's input JSON schema in the row. |
| `--limit int` | Print only the first N tools; `0` prints all (default `0`). A negative value exits with code 2. |

### `mcp gateway call`

Invoke a tool exposed by the gateway and print its raw MCP result.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp gateway call <tool-name> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--args string` | Tool arguments as a JSON object (for example, `'{"id":"abc"}'`). |

Find tool names and their input schemas with `c1i mcp gateway list-tools --full`.

`call` rejects the global `--dry-run` flag.

## MCP servers

Register, configure, and inspect the MCP servers under a C1 app. C1 models an MCP server as a connector under an app, so you address one with `--app-id` plus its connector ID. Hosted servers run inside C1 from a catalog entry; external servers point at a third-party MCP URL. C1 seals auth secrets server-side, so reads only return `*_configured` booleans.

After you register a server, its tools start in the pending-review state. Approve them with [`c1i mcp tools approve`](#mcp-tools) before the gateway proxies calls to them.

| Command | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `c1i mcp servers list --app-id <app-id>` | List MCP servers registered under an app. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| [`c1i mcp servers get`](#mcp-servers-get) | Get a single MCP server. |
| [`c1i mcp servers search`](#mcp-servers-search) | Search MCP servers within an app, with per-server tool counts. |
| [`c1i mcp servers register`](#mcp-servers-register) | Register a new hosted or external MCP server under an existing app. |
| [`c1i mcp servers update`](#mcp-servers-update) | Update an MCP server's editable metadata. |
| [`c1i mcp servers update-credentials`](#mcp-servers-update-credentials) | Replace an MCP server's auth config and config fields. |
| [`c1i mcp servers delete`](#mcp-servers-delete) | Soft-delete an MCP server. |
| [`c1i mcp servers resync-tools`](#mcp-servers-resync-tools) | Re-run tool discovery for an external MCP server. |
| [`c1i mcp servers test-connection`](#mcp-servers-test-connection) | Probe an external MCP server's reachability. |
| [`c1i mcp servers discover-oidc`](#mcp-servers-discover-oidc) | Fetch an issuer's OIDC discovery document. |
| [`c1i mcp servers catalog list`](#mcp-servers-catalog-list) | List the hosted MCP server catalog entries. |
| `c1i mcp servers catalog get <catalog-id>` | Get one catalog entry by its catalog ID, including the full `authModes` and `config_schema` that you need to build a `register` request. Each auth mode carries its own `scopes` (required) and `optionalScopes`. |
| [`c1i mcp servers connections list`](#mcp-servers) | List the calling user's per-user (passthrough) MCP connections. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |

### `mcp servers get`

An MCP server has no `id` field: its identity is `connectorId`, the value this command takes as its argument and that `mcp servers list` rows emit as `connector_id`.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp servers get <connector-id> --app-id <app-id>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID. (Required.) |
