### `mcp servers search`

Search the MCP servers under one app. The server computes a tool count only when you set `--tool-state`, so without it c1i omits `tool_count` from each row instead of showing a `0` that looks like a server with no tools. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp servers search --app-id <app-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID. (Required.) |
| `--query string` | Fuzzy search on display name. |
| `--tool-state string` | Tool state to count: `pending`, `approved`, `disabled`, or `removed`. Without it, `tool_count` is omitted. |
| `--include-last-called-at` | Populate `last_called_at` per row (one extra read per server), to spot idle servers. |

### `mcp servers register`

For a hosted server, browse [`catalog list`](#mcp-servers-catalog-list) and pass an entry's ID with `--catalog-id`. For an external server, pass `--server-url`.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp servers register --app-id <app-id> --type <hosted|external> \
  --display-name <name> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID to register under. (Required unless `--print-config-template`.) |
| `--type string` | Server type: `hosted` or `external`. (Required unless `--print-config-template`.) |
| `--display-name string` | Display name. (Required unless `--print-config-template`.) |
| `--description string` | Description. |
| `--catalog-id string` | Catalog entry ID (hosted). |
| `--source-app-id string` | Source app ID for connector-backed hosted servers. |
| `--config-field stringArray` | Extra config field as `key=value` (hosted, repeatable). |
| `--server-url string` | External MCP server URL (external). |
| `--transport string` | Transport: `streamable-http` or `sse` (external). |
| `--auth string` | Auth method: `none`, `bearer-token`, `custom-header`, or `basic-auth`. OAuth2, AWS, and Google auth go through the `--*-config-file` flags. |
| `--bearer-token string` | Bearer token value (with `--auth bearer-token`). |
| `--header-name string` | Custom header name (with `--auth custom-header`). |
| `--header-value string` | Custom header value (with `--auth custom-header`). |
| `--basic-auth-username string` | Basic-auth username (with `--auth basic-auth`). |
| `--basic-auth-password string` | Basic-auth password (with `--auth basic-auth`). |
| `--hosted-config-file string` | Full `hostedConfig` JSON (file, or `-` for stdin). |
| `--external-config-file string` | Full `externalConfig` JSON (file, or `-` for stdin). |
| `--print-config-template` | Print a ready-to-edit config skeleton for the `--auth` method (use with `--auth` and `--type`) instead of registering. |
| `--token-sharing string` | Token sharing: `shared` or `per-user`. |
| `--data-sensitivity string` | Data sensitivity: `public`, `internal`, `confidential`, or `restricted`. |
| `--tool-prefix string` | Prefix for exposed tool names. |
| `--require-tool-approval` | Per-server override for tool auto-approval (omit to leave unset). |
| `--user-id stringArray` | Integration owner user ID (repeatable). |
| `--wait` | Poll `mcp servers list --app-id` until the new server appears, or `--wait-timeout` elapses. |
| `--wait-timeout duration` | Maximum time to wait with `--wait` (default `1m0s`). |

A list read immediately after registering can omit the server for a second or two while `mcp servers get` already returns it; `--wait` handles that lag. A timeout exits `1`, though the registration was accepted. An explicit `--user-id` is looked up before sending; an unknown user exits with code `4`, including under `--dry-run`.

The `per-user` token-sharing mode works only with OAuth2 (authorization-code or passthrough), bearer-token, custom-header, and basic-auth methods. `register` requires `--app-id`; registering under a new managed app isn't reachable over REST.

**Examples:**

```bash
# Print an OAuth2 config skeleton to edit
c1i mcp servers register --print-config-template --auth oauth2 --type hosted

# Register an external server with a bearer token
c1i mcp servers register --app-id <app-id> --type external \
  --display-name "Example MCP" --server-url https://mcp.example.com/v1 \
  --auth bearer-token --bearer-token <token>
```

### `mcp servers update`

c1i derives the update mask from the flags you set, so `--display-name` alone changes only the name. Auth config and URL aren't updatable here; use `update-credentials`.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp servers update <connector-id> --app-id <app-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID. (Required.) |
| `--display-name string` | New display name. |
| `--description string` | New description. |
| `--data-sensitivity string` | New data sensitivity: `public`, `internal`, `confidential`, or `restricted`. |
| `--tool-prefix string` | New tool-name prefix. |
| `--require-tool-approval` | Per-server override for tool auto-approval. |

### `mcp servers update-credentials`

Replace the auth config, extra config fields, or both on an existing MCP server. It uses the same convenience auth flags and `--*-config-file` flags as `register`, and re-seals secrets server-side.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp servers update-credentials <connector-id> --app-id <app-id> \
  --type <hosted|external> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID. (Required.) |
| `--type string` | Config shape, which must match the server: `hosted` or `external`. (Required.) |
| `--catalog-id string` | Catalog entry ID (hosted). |
| `--source-app-id string` | Source app ID (hosted). |
| `--config-field stringArray` | Extra config field as `key=value` (hosted, repeatable). |
| `--server-url string` | External MCP server URL (external). |
| `--transport string` | Transport: `streamable-http` or `sse` (external). |
| `--auth string` | Auth method: `none`, `bearer-token`, `custom-header`, or `basic-auth`. OAuth2, AWS, and Google auth go through the `--*-config-file` flags. |
| `--bearer-token string` | Bearer token value (with `--auth bearer-token`). |
| `--header-name string` | Custom header name (with `--auth custom-header`). |
| `--header-value string` | Custom header value (with `--auth custom-header`). |
| `--basic-auth-username string` | Basic-auth username (with `--auth basic-auth`). |
| `--basic-auth-password string` | Basic-auth password (with `--auth basic-auth`). |
| `--hosted-config-file string` | Full `hostedConfig` JSON (file, or `-` for stdin). |
| `--external-config-file string` | Full `externalConfig` JSON (file, or `-` for stdin). |
| `--token-sharing string` | Token sharing: `shared` or `per-user`. |
| `--require-tool-approval` | Per-server override for tool auto-approval (omit to leave unset). |
| `--update-mask string` | Comma-separated proto field paths to update (default: derived from the fields you supply). |

The mask uses the auth oneof case name (`bearerToken`, `customHeader`, `basicAuth`, `oauth2`, or `none`), not the `hostedConfig` or `externalConfig` wrapper.

### `mcp servers delete`

This stops the connector, removes it from the tenant's MCP catalog, and revokes any per-user OAuth credentials issued against it. The connector record is retained for audit.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp servers delete <connector-id> --app-id <app-id>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID. (Required.) |

<Warning>
Deleting a server cascades: every toolset bound to the connector, and each toolset's app entitlement, is soft-deleted with it, which affects anyone whose access came through those entitlements. Check what depends on the server first with `c1i mcp toolsets list --app-id <app-id> --connector-id <connector-id>` and `c1i grants list --app-id <app-id> --entitlement-id <entitlement-id>`.
</Warning>

### `mcp servers resync-tools`

Re-run tool discovery against an external MCP server using your own credential. Newly discovered tools land in the pending-review state; approve them with `c1i mcp tools approve`. Hosted servers return `resync is only supported for external MCP servers`.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp servers resync-tools <connector-id> --app-id <app-id>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID. (Required.) |

### `mcp servers test-connection`

Probe whether an external MCP server is reachable with the credentials you supply. Outputs formatted JSON with `reachable` (whether the MCP initialize and `tools/list` calls both succeeded), `toolCount`, and a sanitized `failureReason`. Hosted servers return `test connection is only supported for external MCP servers`. When the server is unreachable, c1i still prints the JSON, then exits with code 8 and says `MCP server is unreachable` on stderr, so check the exit code and not just `reachable`.

The command has two modes. In create mode, supply the config to probe with `--server-url` and optional auth flags, or with `--external-config-file`. In edit mode, pass a connector ID with `--app-id` to probe an existing server, naming any changed fields in `--update-mask`; omit the mask to probe the stored config.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp servers test-connection [<connector-id>] [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID of an existing external server (edit mode). |
| `--server-url string` | External MCP server URL (create mode). |
| `--transport string` | Transport: `streamable-http` or `sse`. |
| `--auth string` | Auth method: `none`, `bearer-token`, `custom-header`, or `basic-auth`. OAuth2, AWS, and Google auth go through `--external-config-file`. |
| `--bearer-token string` | Bearer token value (with `--auth bearer-token`). |
| `--header-name string` | Custom header name (with `--auth custom-header`). |
| `--header-value string` | Custom header value (with `--auth custom-header`). |
| `--basic-auth-username string` | Basic-auth username (with `--auth basic-auth`). |
| `--basic-auth-password string` | Basic-auth password (with `--auth basic-auth`). |
| `--external-config-file string` | Full `externalConfig` JSON (file, or `-` for stdin). |
| `--token-sharing string` | Token sharing: `shared` or `per-user`. |
| `--require-tool-approval` | Per-server override for tool auto-approval (omit to leave unset). |
| `--update-mask string` | Comma-separated changed `externalConfig` paths (edit mode). |

### `mcp servers discover-oidc`

Fetch the OpenID Connect discovery document for an issuer and return its authorization endpoint, token endpoint, supported scopes, and PKCE methods. Use it to fill in the OAuth2 fields of a `register` or `update-credentials` request.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp servers discover-oidc --issuer-url <url>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--issuer-url string` | OIDC issuer base URL. C1 appends `/.well-known/openid-configuration` itself. (Required.) |

### `mcp servers catalog list`

Rows include `service_name`, `base_url`, `default_tool_prefix`, `stable`, and `required_scope_count` and `optional_scope_count`, which tell near-duplicate entries apart. Many services appear twice: once as a thin wrapper over the vendor's REST API, and once as the vendor's own hosted MCP endpoint, whose `service_name` typically ends in `-mcp`. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--query string` | Filter catalog entries by display name. |

## MCP tools

Manage the MCP tools that C1 discovers from a registered server. Newly discovered tools land in the pending-review state, and an admin approves them before the MCP gateway proxies calls to them. Every command requires `--app-id` and `--connector-id` to identify the server.

| Command | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `c1i mcp tools list --app-id <app-id> --connector-id <connector-id>` | List the MCP tools discovered for a connector. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| `c1i mcp tools get <tool-id> --app-id <app-id> --connector-id <connector-id>` | Get a single MCP tool. |
| [`c1i mcp tools search`](#mcp-tools-search) | Search MCP tools with filters. |
| [`c1i mcp tools approve`](#mcp-tools-approve) | Approve one or more MCP tools. |
| [`c1i mcp tools delete`](#mcp-tools-delete) | Soft-delete an MCP tool. |
| [`c1i mcp tools history`](#mcp-tools-history) | Stream the change history for a single MCP tool, newest first. |

### `mcp tools search`

[Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp tools search --app-id <app-id> --connector-id <connector-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--query string` | Fuzzy search on tool name or display name. |
| `--state stringArray` | Filter by state: `pending`, `approved`, `disabled`, or `removed` (repeatable). |
| `--classification stringArray` | Filter by classification: `read`, `write`, `destructive`, `sensitive`, or `dangerous` (repeatable). |

### `mcp tools approve`

Approve MCP tools by setting each tool's state to approved, which lets the MCP gateway proxy calls to them. Pass one or more tool IDs. The API has no batch approve, so c1i sends each ID as its own request and prints one compact JSON line for each applied tool, with its `.id` and `.state`. If one fails, c1i still attempts the rest and exits non-zero with the first failure's code, while the IDs before it stay approved. A failed write to stdout stops the batch with exit code 1. With `--error-format json`, each failed ID gets its own error object, and a summary lists the IDs c1i never sent in `notAttempted`.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp tools approve <tool-id>... --app-id <app-id> \
  --connector-id <connector-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--state string` | Target state: `pending`, `approved`, or `disabled` (default `approved`). |

**Example:**

```bash
# Approve every pending read-only tool on a connector
c1i mcp tools approve $(c1i mcp tools search --app-id <app-id> --connector-id <connector-id> \
  --classification read --state pending --fields id | jq -r .id) \
  --app-id <app-id> --connector-id <connector-id>
```

### `mcp tools delete`

The tool stays in the database for audit but is hidden from listings and unbound from any toolsets. Tools move to the removed state on their own during sync when they disappear from the upstream server; to block a tool without deleting it, use `c1i mcp tools approve --state disabled`.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp tools delete <tool-id> --app-id <app-id> --connector-id <connector-id>
```

### `mcp tools history`

Outputs NDJSON, one record per historical version. Each record carries the full tool snapshot at that point and the change-history envelope (actor, change kind, creation time, trace ID, system log event ID, and annotations). [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits); `--page-size` accepts up to 200.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp tools history <tool-id> --app-id <app-id> \
  --connector-id <connector-id> [flags]
```

## MCP prompts

Review the MCP prompts a connector's server exposes. Prompt metadata comes from discovery, and the API spec says only state is editable, so the only change these commands make is the approval state, with `mcp prompts approve`. There is no `update` and no `delete`. Every command takes `--app-id` and `--connector-id` to identify the server.

A tenant without the prompts feature answers a well-formed prompts request with a `403 feature not enabled` (exit `3`); a malformed ID gets the server's `400` first (exit `2`).

| Command | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| [`c1i mcp prompts list`](#mcp-prompts-list) | List the prompts stored for a connector. |
| [`c1i mcp prompts search`](#mcp-prompts-search) | Search prompts by text and state. |
| [`c1i mcp prompts get`](#mcp-prompts-get) | Get one prompt's metadata and argument definitions. |
| [`c1i mcp prompts history`](#mcp-prompts-history) | Stream the change history for a single prompt. |
| [`c1i mcp prompts approve`](#mcp-prompts-approve) | Set one or more prompts' approval state. |

### `mcp prompts list`

List the MCP prompts stored for a connector. Use [`search`](#mcp-prompts-search) to filter by state or text. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp prompts list --app-id <app-id> --connector-id <connector-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID. (Required.) |
| `--connector-id string` | Connector ID. (Required.) |

Rows are `id`, `app_id`, `connector_id`, `name`, `title`, `description`, `state`, `app_entitlement_id`, `argument_names` (the prompt's argument names, in order), and `deleted_at`.

### `mcp prompts search`

Search a connector's MCP prompts by text and state. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp prompts search --app-id <app-id> --connector-id <connector-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID. (Required.) |
| `--connector-id string` | Connector ID. (Required.) |
| `--query string` | Text matched against name, title, and description. |
| `--state stringArray` | Filter by state (repeatable; default pending, approved, and disabled): `pending`, `approved`, `disabled`, `removed`, or any `MCP_PROMPT_STATE_*` value. |
| `--include-grant-status` | Add `entitlement_active` and `grant_count` to each prompt row. |

The API spec says an empty state filter "selects PENDING_REVIEW, APPROVED and DISABLED; request REMOVED explicitly", so removed prompts appear only with `--state removed`. Without `--include-grant-status` those two keys are omitted from each row, because the server doesn't compute them.

### `mcp prompts get`

Get one MCP prompt's stored metadata and its argument definitions, in discovery order. The prompt isn't rendered.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp prompts get <prompt-id> --app-id <app-id> --connector-id <connector-id>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID. (Required.) |
| `--connector-id string` | Connector ID. (Required.) |

### `mcp prompts history`

Outputs NDJSON, one record per historical version of the prompt, newest first as the server returns them: a snapshot of the prompt plus change metadata. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp prompts history <prompt-id> --app-id <app-id> \
  --connector-id <connector-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID. (Required.) |
| `--connector-id string` | Connector ID. (Required.) |

### `mcp prompts approve`

Approve MCP prompts by setting each one's state to `MCP_PROMPT_STATE_APPROVED`, or to another state with `--state`. The API spec says "Only state is editable", which is why there is no separate update command; it also says "Approval changes converge asynchronously to access grants; success confirms the saved state", so a `200` confirms the stored state, not that every grant has caught up.

Pass one or more prompt IDs; they all share `--app-id`, `--connector-id`, and `--state`. Each ID is its own request and prints one compact JSON line — the prompt, with `.id` and `.state` — on stdout in argument order. If one fails the rest are still attempted and the command exits with the first failure's code, so it can exit non-zero with some prompts already changed: with `--error-format json`, check each per-ID error's `applied` and the summary's `notAttempted`.

`--state=removed` is refused with exit `2` and nothing sent: sync sets that state when a prompt disappears from the upstream server. Use `--state disabled` to block a prompt instead.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp prompts approve <prompt-id>... --app-id <app-id> \
  --connector-id <connector-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID. (Required.) |
| `--connector-id string` | Connector ID. (Required.) |
| `--state string` | Target state: `pending`, `approved`, or `disabled` (default `approved`). |

## MCP resources

Review the MCP resources a connector's server exposes. Resources are discovered by sync, and the API spec calls resource metadata "discovery-owned and read-only", so the only change these commands make is the approval state, with `mcp resources approve`. There is no `update` and no `delete`. Every command takes `--app-id` and `--connector-id` to identify the server.

| Command | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| [`c1i mcp resources list`](#mcp-resources-list) | List every resource stored for a connector. |
| [`c1i mcp resources search`](#mcp-resources-search) | Search resources by text, state, and kind. |
| [`c1i mcp resources get`](#mcp-resources-get) | Get one resource's state, kind, URI, and MIME type. |
| [`c1i mcp resources history`](#mcp-resources-history) | Stream the change history for a single resource. |
| [`c1i mcp resources approve`](#mcp-resources-approve) | Set one or more resources' approval state. |

### `mcp resources list`

List every MCP resource stored for a connector, in every state including `removed` — sync marks a resource removed when the upstream server stops exposing it. Use [`search`](#mcp-resources-search) to filter by state or kind. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp resources list --app-id <app-id> --connector-id <connector-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID. (Required.) |
| `--connector-id string` | Connector ID. (Required.) |

Rows are `id`, `app_id`, `connector_id`, `name`, `title`, `description`, `kind`, `uri`, `uri_template`, `mime_type`, `state`, `app_entitlement_id`, and `deleted_at`. A static resource fills `uri`; a resource template fills `uri_template`.

### `mcp resources search`

Search a connector's MCP resources by text, state, and kind. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits).

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp resources search --app-id <app-id> --connector-id <connector-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID. (Required.) |
| `--connector-id string` | Connector ID. (Required.) |
| `--query string` | Text matched against name, title, description, and URI. |
| `--state stringArray` | Filter by state (repeatable; default pending, approved, and disabled): `pending`, `approved`, `disabled`, `removed`, or any `MCP_RESOURCE_STATE_*` value. |
| `--kind stringArray` | Filter by kind (repeatable): `static`, `template`, or any `MCP_RESOURCE_KIND_*` value. |
| `--include-grant-status` | Add `entitlement_active` and `grant_count` to each resource row. |

Without `--state` the server returns pending, approved, and disabled resources; removed ones appear only with `--state removed`. Without `--include-grant-status` those two keys are omitted from each row, because the server doesn't compute them.

### `mcp resources get`

Get one MCP resource: its approval state, kind, URI or URI template, MIME type, and bound `app_entitlement_id`.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp resources get <resource-id> --app-id <app-id> \
  --connector-id <connector-id>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID. (Required.) |
| `--connector-id string` | Connector ID. (Required.) |

### `mcp resources history`

Outputs NDJSON, one record per historical version of the resource, newest first as the server returns them: `{"snapshot": <the resource>, "metadata": {"actor", "changeKind", "createdAt", "traceId", ...}}`. An unknown resource ID prints nothing and exits `0`, because the server answers with an empty list — this matches [`mcp tools history`](#mcp-tools-history). [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits); `--page-size` accepts up to 200.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp resources history <resource-id> --app-id <app-id> \
  --connector-id <connector-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID. (Required.) |
| `--connector-id string` | Connector ID. (Required.) |

### `mcp resources approve`

Approve MCP resources by setting each one's state to `MCP_RESOURCE_STATE_APPROVED`, or to another state with `--state`. State is the only field the API lets you change on a resource, which is why there is no separate update command.

Pass one or more resource IDs; they all share `--app-id`, `--connector-id`, and `--state`. Each ID is its own request and prints one compact JSON line — the resource, with `.id` and `.state` — on stdout in argument order. If one fails the rest are still attempted and the command exits with the first failure's code, so it can exit non-zero with some resources already changed: with `--error-format json`, check each per-ID error's `applied` and the summary's `notAttempted`.

`--state=removed` is refused with exit `2` and nothing sent: sync sets that state when a resource disappears from the upstream server. Use `--state disabled` to block a resource instead.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp resources approve <resource-id>... --app-id <app-id> \
  --connector-id <connector-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--app-id string` | Application ID. (Required.) |
| `--connector-id string` | Connector ID. (Required.) |
| `--state string` | Target state: `pending`, `approved`, or `disabled` (default `approved`). |

**Example:**

```bash
# Approve every pending resource on a connector
c1i mcp resources approve $(c1i mcp resources search --app-id <app-id> \
  --connector-id <connector-id> --state pending --fields id | jq -r .id) \
  --app-id <app-id> --connector-id <connector-id>
```

## MCP toolsets

Manage MCP toolsets: admin-curated groupings of tools, each bound to a single app entitlement so users can request and receive the whole bundle at once. Every command except `get-by-entitlement` and `requestable-connectors` requires `--app-id` and `--connector-id` to identify the server; `get-by-entitlement` requires only `--app-id`.

| Command | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `c1i mcp toolsets list --app-id <app-id> --connector-id <connector-id>` | List MCP toolsets for a connector. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| `c1i mcp toolsets get <toolset-id> --app-id <app-id> --connector-id <connector-id>` | Get a single MCP toolset. |
| [`c1i mcp toolsets get-by-entitlement`](#mcp-toolsets-get-by-entitlement) | Resolve a toolset by its app entitlement ID. |
| [`c1i mcp toolsets create`](#mcp-toolsets-create) | Create an MCP toolset. |
| [`c1i mcp toolsets update`](#mcp-toolsets-update) | Update a toolset's display name, description, or both. |
| `c1i mcp toolsets delete <toolset-id> --app-id <app-id> --connector-id <connector-id>` | Soft-delete an MCP toolset. |
| `c1i mcp toolsets requestable-connectors <user-id>` | For a user, list the app and connector ID pairs that have at least one toolset the user can currently request access to. Unlike most list commands, this endpoint isn't paginated server-side: it returns the full set in one response. |

### `mcp toolsets get-by-entitlement`

Look up the toolset that owns a given app entitlement. Each toolset creates one app entitlement at sync time, so this reverse lookup finds which toolset an entitlement ID from an access-request task represents.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp toolsets get-by-entitlement <app-entitlement-id> --app-id <app-id>
```

### `mcp toolsets create`

Create an MCP toolset (access profile) under a connector. After creating it, attach tools with [`c1i mcp bindings create`](#mcp-bindings).

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp toolsets create --app-id <app-id> --connector-id <connector-id> \
  --display-name <name> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--display-name string` | Toolset display name. (Required.) |
| `--description string` | Toolset description. |

### `mcp toolsets update`

c1i derives the update mask from the flags you set. Editing the app entitlement behind the toolset isn't supported here.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp toolsets update <toolset-id> --app-id <app-id> \
  --connector-id <connector-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--display-name string` | New display name (omit to leave unchanged). |
| `--description string` | New description (omit to leave unchanged). |

## MCP bindings

Inspect and manage bindings between MCP tools and MCP toolsets (access profiles): which tools are members of which toolset. This is a different object from an entitlement proxy binding (entitlement to entitlement), which you manage with [`c1i entitlements proxy-bindings`](#entitlements-proxy-bindings). Every command requires `--app-id` and `--connector-id` to identify the server.

| Command | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `c1i mcp bindings list --app-id <app-id> --connector-id <connector-id> --toolset-id <toolset-id>` | List the tool bindings of an MCP toolset (access profile). [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| [`c1i mcp bindings by-tools`](#mcp-bindings-by-tools) | List the toolsets each given tool is bound to. |
| [`c1i mcp bindings create`](#mcp-bindings-create) | Bind one or more MCP tools to a toolset. |
| [`c1i mcp bindings delete`](#mcp-bindings-delete) | Remove one or more MCP tools from a toolset. |
| [`c1i mcp bindings history`](#mcp-bindings-history) | Stream binding-change history for a toolset or a tool. |

### `mcp bindings by-tools`

Reverse lookup: given a batch of MCP tool IDs, emit one NDJSON record per tool with the toolsets it's bound to. Tools with no bindings are still emitted, with an empty `toolsets` array.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp bindings by-tools --app-id <app-id> --connector-id <connector-id> \
  --tool-id <tool-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--tool-id stringArray` | MCP tool ID to look up (repeatable; maximum 32). (Required.) |

### `mcp bindings create`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp bindings create --app-id <app-id> --connector-id <connector-id> \
  --toolset-id <toolset-id> --tool-id <tool-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--toolset-id string` | MCP toolset (access profile) ID. (Required.) |
| `--tool-id stringArray` | MCP tool ID to bind (repeatable; maximum 100). (Required.) |

### `mcp bindings delete`

The tool IDs travel in the request body, so c1i uses the `tool_bindings/delete` action route rather than an HTTP `DELETE`.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp bindings delete --app-id <app-id> --connector-id <connector-id> \
  --toolset-id <toolset-id> --tool-id <tool-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--toolset-id string` | MCP toolset (access profile) ID. (Required.) |
| `--tool-id stringArray` | MCP tool ID to unbind (repeatable; maximum 100). (Required.) |

### `mcp bindings history`

Stream the binding-change history for either all tools bound to one toolset (`--toolset-id`) or all toolsets that contain one tool (`--tool-id`). Pass exactly one of the two. Outputs NDJSON, newest first. Each record carries a `list_history` metadata envelope plus the items added or removed in that transaction. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits); `--page-size` accepts up to 200.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp bindings history --app-id <app-id> --connector-id <connector-id> \
  (--toolset-id <toolset-id> | --tool-id <tool-id>) [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--toolset-id string` | MCP toolset (access profile) ID. |
| `--tool-id string` | MCP tool ID. |

## MCP classifiers

Manage the AI-governance classifier surfaces used with the MCP gateway (formerly called guardrails in c1i's user-facing CLI).

A classifier is a reusable, ordered rule cascade evaluated first-match-wins. Bind a classifier to an enforcement surface with `bindings create`; rules may reference tool gates. Templates instantiate curated classifiers (and their dependent gates) into a tenant-owned, editable classifier. The singleton `policy` surface — the tenant's one agent classifier policy — is separate from named classifiers.

`create` and `update` take JSON objects through `--body-file` (or `-` for stdin), preserving nested CEL rules, hooks, gates, and template parameters without a lossy flag translation. Updates require an explicit `--update-mask` as a safeguard — including `rules` in the mask replaces the entire ordered cascade.

| Command | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `c1i mcp classifiers list` | List classifiers. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| `c1i mcp classifiers get <classifier-id>` | Get a classifier by ID. |
| [`c1i mcp classifiers create`](#mcp-classifiers-create) | Create a named, reusable classifier from JSON. |
| [`c1i mcp classifiers update`](#mcp-classifiers-update) | Update selected classifier fields from a JSON object. |
| `c1i mcp classifiers delete <classifier-id>` | Delete a classifier. |
| `c1i mcp classifiers bindings list` | List classifier bindings. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| [`c1i mcp classifiers bindings create`](#mcp-classifiers-bindings-create) | Bind a classifier to an enforcement surface. |
| `c1i mcp classifiers bindings delete <binding-id>` | Delete a classifier binding. |
| `c1i mcp classifiers templates list [--latest-only]` | List classifier templates; `--latest-only` returns only the latest revision of each. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| `c1i mcp classifiers templates get <template-id> [--template-version <version>]` | Get a classifier template revision (default: the latest version). |
| [`c1i mcp classifiers templates instantiate`](#mcp-classifiers-templates-instantiate) | Instantiate a template into a tenant-owned, editable classifier. |
| [`c1i mcp classifiers templates add-rule`](#mcp-classifiers-templates-add-rule) | Insert one rule from a template into a classifier. |
| `c1i mcp classifiers policy show` | Show the tenant's singleton agent classifier policy. |
| [`c1i mcp classifiers policy update`](#mcp-classifiers-policy-update) | Update selected singleton policy fields from JSON. |
| `c1i mcp classifiers tool-gates list` | List tool gates. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| `c1i mcp classifiers tool-gates search [--query <text>]` | Search tool gates by text; without `--query`, it returns all tool gates. [Paginates](#pagination-and-limits). |
| `c1i mcp classifiers tool-gates get <tool-gate-id>` | Get a tool gate by ID. |
| [`c1i mcp classifiers tool-gates create`](#mcp-classifiers-tool-gates-create) | Create a tool gate from a complete JSON request object. |
| [`c1i mcp classifiers tool-gates update`](#mcp-classifiers-tool-gates-update) | Update selected tool-gate fields from a JSON object. |
| `c1i mcp classifiers tool-gates delete <tool-gate-id>` | Delete a tool gate. |

### `mcp classifiers create`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp classifiers create --body-file <file>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--body-file string` | Classifier JSON object — not the create-request wrapper (file, or `-` for stdin). (Required.) |

`displayName` is required. Rules are evaluated in listed order, first match wins; leave a new rule's `id` empty for the server to assign it.

### `mcp classifiers update`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp classifiers update <classifier-id> --body-file <file> \
  --update-mask <fields>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--body-file string` | Complete classifier JSON object (file, or `-` for stdin). (Required.) |
| `--update-mask string` | Comma-separated camelCase field paths to update, for example `description,defaultOutcome`. (Required.) |

The server requires `displayName` even when it's not in the mask. The positional ID overrides any `id` in the file. Including `rules` in the mask replaces every rule in evaluation order — read and preserve the existing classifier first if you're only changing some rules.

### `mcp classifiers bindings create`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp classifiers bindings create --body-file <file>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--body-file string` | Classifier-binding JSON object — not the create-request wrapper (file, or `-` for stdin). (Required.) |

`surface` and `classifierId` are required; `targetId` identifies the applicable agent or gateway target when the selected surface uses one.

### `mcp classifiers templates instantiate`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp classifiers templates instantiate <template-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--body-file string` | Optional JSON object: `templateVersion`, `displayName`, and `params` (file, or `-` for stdin). |

Required params are declared by `templates get` and validated by the server.

### `mcp classifiers templates add-rule`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp classifiers templates add-rule <classifier-id> <template-id> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--body-file string` | Optional JSON object: `templateVersion`, `ruleIndex`, `insertIndex`, and `params` (file, or `-` for stdin). |

`ruleIndex` defaults to `0`; an `insertIndex` at or past the end of the cascade appends the rule.

### `mcp classifiers policy update`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp classifiers policy update --body-file <file> --update-mask <fields>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--body-file string` | Partial agent-classifier-policy JSON object (file, or `-` for stdin). (Required.) |
| `--update-mask string` | Comma-separated camelCase field paths to update. (Required.) |

The policy is implicitly created by its first update. Including `rules` in the mask replaces the entire first-match-wins cascade with the order supplied.

### `mcp classifiers tool-gates create`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp classifiers tool-gates create --body-file <file>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--body-file string` | Full tool-gate create-request JSON object (file, or `-` for stdin). (Required.) |

`displayName` and `grantPolicyId` are required. `filter` may set a CEL expression or a built-in pattern, but never both — the server resolves a built-in pattern to its own CEL expression.

### `mcp classifiers tool-gates update`

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i mcp classifiers tool-gates update <tool-gate-id> --body-file <file> \
  --update-mask <fields>
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--body-file string` | Complete tool-gate JSON object (file, or `-` for stdin). (Required.) |
| `--update-mask string` | Comma-separated camelCase field paths to update. (Required.) |

## API

Make raw C1 API requests. This is an escape hatch for accessing any API endpoint not covered by the built-in commands.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i api --path <path> [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--path string` | API endpoint path. (Required.) |
| `--method string` | HTTP method: `GET`, `POST`, `PUT`, `PATCH`, or `DELETE`. (Default: `GET`, or `POST` if a body is set.) |
| `--body string` | JSON request body (implies `POST`). |
| `--body-file string` | Read the JSON request body from a file (`-` for stdin); mutually exclusive with `--body`. |
| `--allow-delete-body` | Allow `--body` or `--body-file` with `--method DELETE`. Some endpoints, such as remove-membership, require a body on `DELETE`; without this flag c1i refuses the request. |
| `--header stringArray` | Extra request header as `key=value` (repeatable). |
| `--query stringArray` | Query parameter as `key=value` (repeatable). |
| `--paginate` | Automatically follow pagination to fetch all pages, and output results as NDJSON. |
| `--list-key string` | Force the response field name to drain as the list (default: the first array-valued field, such as `list`, `automationExecutions`, or `automations`). A misspelled key exits with code 2, and the error names the page's array fields. |
| `--limit int` | With `--paginate`, cap the total number of NDJSON rows emitted (0 = unlimited). Errors if used without `--paginate`. |

`c1i api` returns one response unless `--paginate` is set. A `nextPageToken` in a response fetched without `--paginate` is a partial result: c1i warns on stderr that more pages exist and that it is discarding the cursor, rather than silently printing only the first page. With `--paginate`, a `pageToken` in the request body (or `page_token` in `--query`) is the first page's cursor and later pages replace it, while `pageSize` is sent as given.

**Examples:**

```bash
# POST request — passing --body switches the method to POST automatically
c1i api --path /api/v1/search/tasks --body '{"taskStates":["TASK_STATE_OPEN"]}'

# Paginate a POST search endpoint
c1i api --path /api/v1/search/tasks --body '{"taskStates":["TASK_STATE_OPEN"]}' --paginate
```

If you call a POST-only endpoint with GET (for example a `/search/*` path with no `--body`), c1i prints a one-line hint suggesting `--body` or `--method=POST` so you can recover without consulting the schema.

<Note>
c1i sends `--body` to the API verbatim — it does not wrap or reshape the payload. If the endpoint's schema nests fields under a top-level key (for example, `POST /api/v1/iam/personal_clients/{id}` expects `{"client": {"displayName": "...", "scopedRoles": ["..."]}, "updateMask": "displayName,scopedRoles"}`), your `--body` must include that wrapper. Use `c1i docs endpoint <path>` to see the exact request shape before calling.
</Note>

## Pagination and limits

Commands marked "Paginates" (and `c1i api --paginate`) auto-paginate by default — they keep fetching pages until the cursor is empty, so you don't have to manage tokens manually.

Each of those commands takes three flags that control how much data flows:

| Flag | Effect |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--page-size N` | Rows to request per API page (max 100). The server may return more than asked, so use `--limit` for an exact count. Default is 50. `policies search` defaults to 25. The history commands (`mcp tools history`, `mcp resources history`, `mcp bindings history`) accept up to 200, `connectors debuggable` up to 50, and `mcp prompts history` caps at 100. A value above the maximum is clamped, not an error, and `--page-size 0` asks for the server's default. |
| `--page-token TOKEN` | Resume from a specific cursor. When set, c1i fetches a single page and exits — auto-pagination is disabled. |
| `--limit N` | Cap the *total* number of results emitted across all pages (0 = unlimited). Auto-pagination stops fetching new pages once the cap is reached, and c1i tightens the per-call request size when `--limit` is smaller than `--page-size` so it doesn't over-fetch. |

**Examples:**

```bash
# Peek at the first 5 users without fetching everyone
c1i users list --limit 5

# Cap a wide query — useful for cost control or quick previews
c1i tasks list --state open --limit 25
```

A blank `--page-token` (empty, whitespace-only, or invisible-only) exits with code 2. Without that check, an unset shell variable would turn off auto-pagination and return only the first page.

If a server's pagination cursor stops advancing, c1i doesn't loop forever. A cursor counts as stuck when `nextPageToken` repeats, when two or more pages in a row (25 or more rows in all) hold only rows c1i has already seen, or after 100 empty pages in a row. c1i keeps the rows it already printed, warns on stderr that results are INCOMPLETE, and exits with code 6. A list page that is HTML, an array, empty, or truncated also exits with code 6.

A few list commands send one request instead of paginating, and warn on stderr with `partial result` when the server reports more pages: `role-mining suggestions|runs|custom-analysis list`, `findings routing-rules|transformation-rules list`, `apps routing-rules list`, `attribute-types list`, `bridges services`, `grants accounts`, and `settings ai-governance history`.

`c1i api --path X --limit N` without `--paginate` errors immediately — the non-paginated path returns a single response, so there's nothing to cap.

## Version

Print the c1i version.

You can also use `c1i --version`.

## Completion

Generate a shell completion script. Output is the script itself, which you can write to a file or `source` directly. Supported shells are bash, zsh, fish, and PowerShell.

**Usage:**

```bash
c1i completion <shell>
```

**Examples:**

```bash
# bash
c1i completion bash > ~/.local/share/bash-completion/completions/c1i

# zsh
c1i completion zsh > "${fpath[1]}/_c1i"

# fish
c1i completion fish > ~/.config/fish/completions/c1i.fish

# PowerShell (current session; add the line to your $PROFILE to keep it)
c1i completion powershell | Out-String | Invoke-Expression
```

| Flag | Description |
| :--- | :--- |
| `--no-descriptions` | Disable completion descriptions. |

For shell-specific install steps, run `c1i completion <shell> --help`.
